for every open set
\Omega\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{n}
.

Example 24
.

(i)

Let
\delta\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GC}^{\infty}({\text{\rm c}}({\mathbb{R}^{n}}),\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}})
and
H\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GC}^{\infty}({\text{\rm c}}({\mathbb{R}}),\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}})

be the
\iota^{b}
-embeddings of the Dirac delta and of the Heaviside

\delta(x)=b^{n}\cdot\psi(b\cdot x)
, where
\psi(x):=[\psi_{\varepsilon}(x_{\varepsilon})]

is called
n

is an even function because of Lem.

.
(i)
.

if

for some

.
(i)

.

Similar properties can be stated e.g. for

Using these formulas, we can simply consider
\delta\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GC}^{\infty}(\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{n},\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}})

.

.

2.7.
Functionally compact sets and multidimensional integration

2.7.1.
Extreme value theorem and functionally
compact sets

For GSF, suitable generalizations of many classical theorems of differential
and integral calculus hold: intermediate value theorem, mean value
theorems, suitable sheaf properties, local and global inverse function
theorems, Banach fixed point theorem and a corresponding Picard-Lindelöf
theorem both for ODE and PDE, see
[
25
,
26
,
27
,
46
,
28
]
.

Even though the intervals
[a,b]\subseteq\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}
,
a
,
b\in\mathbb{R}
,
are not compact in the sharp topology (see
[
24
]
), analogously
to the case of smooth functions, a GSF satisfies an extreme value
theorem on such sets. In fact, we have:

Theorem 25
.

Let
f\in\mathcal{G}\mathcal{C}^{\infty}(X,\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}})
be a GSF defined
on the subset
X
of
\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
. Let
\emptyset\neq K=[K_{\varepsilon}]\subseteq X

be an internal set generated by a sharply bounded net
(K_{\varepsilon})

of compact sets
K_{\varepsilon}\Subset\mathbb{R}^{n}
, then

\exists m,M\in K\,\forall x\in K:\ f(m)\leq f(x)\leq f(M).

(2.8)

We shall use the assumptions on
K
and
(K_{\varepsilon})
given in this
theorem to introduce a notion of “compact subset” which behaves
better than the usual classical notion of compactness in the sharp
topology.

Definition 26
.

A subset
K
of
\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
is called

functionally compact
, denoted by
K\Subset_{\text{\rm f}}\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
, if there
exists a net
(K_{\varepsilon})
such that

(i)

K=[K_{\varepsilon}]\subseteq\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
.

(ii)

\exists R\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}:\ K\subseteq B_{R}(0)
,
i.e.
K
is sharply bounded.

(iii)

\forall\varepsilon\in I:\ K_{\varepsilon}\Subset\mathbb{R}^{n}
.

If, in addition,
K\subseteq U\subseteq\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
then we write

K\Subset_{\text{\rm f}}U
. Finally, we write
[K_{\varepsilon}]\Subset_{\text{\rm f}}U
if
(ii)
,

(iii)
and
[K_{\varepsilon}]\subseteq U
hold.
Any net
(K_{\varepsilon})
such that
[K_{\varepsilon}]=K
is called a
representative

of
K
.

We motivate the name
functionally compact subset

by noting that on this type of subsets, GSF have properties very similar
to those that ordinary smooth functions have on standard compact sets.

Remark 27
.

(i)

By Thm.
11
.
(iii)
,
any internal set
K=[K_{\varepsilon}]
is closed in the sharp topology and
hence functionally compact sets are always closed. In particular,
the open interval
(0,1)\subseteq\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}
is not functionally compact
since it is not closed.

(ii)

If
H\Subset\mathbb{R}^{n}
is
a non-empty ordinary compact set, then the internal set
[H]
is
functionally compact. In particular,
[0,1]=\left[[0,1]_{\mathbb{R}}\right]

is functionally compact.

(iii)

The empty set
\emptyset=\widetilde{\emptyset}\Subset_{\text{\rm f}}\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}
.

(iv)

\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
is not functionally
compact since it is not sharply bounded.

(v)

The set of compactly
supported points
{\text{\rm c}}({\mathbb{R}})
is not functionally compact because the
GSF
f(x)=x
does not satisfy the conclusion (
2.8
)
of Thm.
25
.

In the present paper, we need the following properties of
functionally compact sets.

Theorem 28
.

(i)

Let
K\subseteq X\subseteq\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
,
f\in\mathcal{G}\mathcal{C}^{\infty}(X,\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{d})
.
Then
K\Subset_{\text{\rm f}}\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
implies
f(K)\Subset_{\text{\rm f}}\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
.

(ii)

Let
K
,
H\Subset_{\text{\rm f}}\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
. If
K\cup H

is an internal set, then it is a functionally compact set. If
K\cap H

is an internal set, then it is a functionally compact set.

(iii)

Let
H\subseteq K\Subset_{\text{\rm f}}\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
, then if
H

is an internal set, then
H\Subset_{\text{\rm f}}\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
.

As a corollary of this theorem and Rem. (
27
).
(ii)

we get

Corollary 29
.

If
a
,
b\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}
and
a\leq b
,
then
[a,b]\Subset_{\text{\rm f}}\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}
.

Let us note that
a
,
b\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}
can also be infinite
numbers, e.g.
a=\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}^{-N}
,
b=\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}^{-M}
or
a=-\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}^{-N}
,

b=\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}^{-M}
with
M>N
, so that e.g.
[-\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}^{-N},\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}^{-M}]\supseteq\mathbb{R}
.
Finally, in the following result we consider the product of functionally
compact sets:

Theorem 30
.

Let
K\Subset_{\text{\rm f}}\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
and
H\Subset_{\text{\rm f}}\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
,
then
K\times H\Subset_{\text{\rm f}}\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{n+d}
. In particular, if
a_{i}\leq b_{i}

for
i=1,\ldots,n
, then
\prod_{i=1}^{n}[a_{i},b_{i}]\Subset_{\text{\rm f}}\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
.

Applying the extreme value theorem Thm.
25

to the first derivative, we also have the following

Theorem 31
.

Let
a
,
b\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
,
a<b
,
f\in{}^{\rho}\mathcal{G}\mathcal{C}^{\infty}([a,b],\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}})

be a GSF. Then

(i)

\exists c\in[a,b]:\ f(b)-f(a)=(b-a)\cdot f^{\prime}(c)
.

(ii)

Setting
M:=\max_{c\in[a,b]}\left|f^{\prime}(c)\right|\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}
,
we hence have
\forall x,y\in[a,b]:\ \left|f(x)-f(y)\right|\leq M\cdot\left|x-y\right|
.

A theory of compactly supported GSF has been developed in
[
24
]
,
and it closely resembles the classical theory of LF-spaces of compactly
supported smooth functions.

2.7.2.
Multidimensional integration

Finally, to define FT of multivariable GSF we have to introduce multidimensional
integration on suitable subsets of
\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
(see
[
27
]
).

Definition 32
.

Let
\mu
be a measure on
\mathbb{R}^{n}
and
let
K
be a functionally compact subset of
\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
. Then,
we call
K

\mu
-
measurable
if the limit

\mu(K):=\lim_{m\to\infty}[\mu(\overline{B^{{\scriptscriptstyle\text{\rm E}}}}_{\rho_{\varepsilon}^{m}}(K_{\varepsilon}))]

(2.9)

exists for some representative
(K_{\varepsilon})
of
K
. Here
m\in\mathbb{N}
,
the limit is taken in the sharp topology on
\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}
, and
\overline{B^{{\scriptscriptstyle\text{\rm E}}}}_{r}(A):=\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}:d(x,A)\leq r\}
.

Let
K\Subset_{\text{\rm f}}\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
. Let
(\Omega_{\varepsilon})

be a net of open subsets of
\mathbb{R}^{n}
, and
(f_{\varepsilon})
be a net
of continuous maps
f_{\varepsilon}
:
\Omega_{\varepsilon}\longrightarrow\mathbb{R}
.
Then we say that

(f_{\varepsilon})\textit{ defines a generalized integrable map}:K\longrightarrow\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}

if

(i)

K\subseteq\langle\Omega_{\varepsilon}\rangle
and
[f_{\varepsilon}(x_{\varepsilon})]\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}

for all
[x_{\varepsilon}]\in K
.

(ii)

\forall(x_{\varepsilon}),(x^{\prime}_{\varepsilon})\in\mathbb{R}_{\rho}^{n}:\ [x_{\varepsilon}]=[x^{\prime}_{\varepsilon}]\in K\ \Rightarrow\ (f_{\varepsilon}(x_{\varepsilon}))\sim_{\rho}(f_{\varepsilon}(x^{\prime}_{\varepsilon}))
.

If
f\in\Set(K,\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}})
is such that

\forall[x_{\varepsilon}]\in K:\ f\left([x_{\varepsilon}]\right)=\left[f_{\varepsilon}(x_{\varepsilon})\right]

(2.10)

we say that
f:K\longrightarrow\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}
is a
generalized
integrable function
.

We will again say that
f

is defined by the net

(f_{\varepsilon})
or that the net
(f_{\varepsilon})

represents

f
.
The set of all these generalized integrable functions will be denoted
by
\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GI}(K,\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}})
.

E.g., if
f=[f_{\varepsilon}(-)]|_{K}\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GC}^{\infty}(K,\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}})
, then
both
f
and
|f|=[|f_{\varepsilon}(-)|]|_{K}
are integrable on
K
(but
note that, in general,
|f|
is not a GSF).

In the following result, we show that this definition generates
a correct notion of multidimensional integration for GSF.

Theorem 33
.

Let
K\subseteq\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{n}

be
\mu
-measurable.

(i)

The definition of
\mu(K)
is independent of
the representative
(K_{\varepsilon})
.

(ii)

There exists a representative
(K_{\varepsilon})

of
K
such that
\mu(K)=[\mu(K_{\varepsilon})]
.

(iii)

Let
(K_{\varepsilon})
be any representative
of
K
and let
f=[f_{\varepsilon}(-)]|_{K}\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GI}(K,\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}})
. Then

\int_{K}f\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\mu}{\hbox{\rm d}\mu}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\mu$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\mu$}}:=\lim_{m\to\infty}\biggl[\int_{\overline{B^{{\scriptscriptstyle\text{\rm E}}}}_{\rho_{\varepsilon}^{m}}(K_{\varepsilon})}f_{\varepsilon}\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\mu}{\hbox{\rm d}\mu}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\mu$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\mu$}}\biggr]\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}

exists and its value is independent of the representative
(K_{\varepsilon})
.

(iv)

There exists a representative
(K_{\varepsilon})

of
K
such that

\int_{K}f\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\mu}{\hbox{\rm d}\mu}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\mu$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\mu$}}=\biggl[\int_{K_{\varepsilon}}f_{\varepsilon}\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\mu}{\hbox{\rm d}\mu}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\mu$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\mu$}}\biggr]\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}

(2.11)

for each
f=[f_{\varepsilon}(-)]|_{K}\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GI}(K,\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}})
. From (
2.11
),
it also follows that
\left|\int_{K}f\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\mu}{\hbox{\rm d}\mu}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\mu$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\mu$}}\right|\leq\int_{K}\left|f\right|\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\mu}{\hbox{\rm d}\mu}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\mu$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\mu$}}
.

(v)

If
K=\prod_{i=1}^{n}[a_{i},b_{i}]
, then

K
is
\lambda
-measurable (
\lambda
being the Lebesgue measure
on
\mathbb{R}^{n}
) and for all for each
f=[f_{\varepsilon}(-)]|_{K}\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GI}(K,\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}})

we have

\int_{K}f\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\lambda}{\hbox{\rm d}\lambda}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\lambda$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\lambda$}}=\left[\int_{a_{1,\varepsilon}}^{b_{1,\varepsilon}}\,dx_{1}\dots\int_{a_{n,\varepsilon}}^{b_{n,\varepsilon}}f_{\varepsilon}(x_{1},\dots,x_{n})\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}x_{n}}{\hbox{\rm d}x_{n}}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}x_{n}$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}x_{n}$}}\right]\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}

(2.12)

for any representatives
(a_{i,\varepsilon})
,
(b_{i,\varepsilon})
of
a_{i}

and
b_{i}
respectively. Therefore, if
n=1
, this notion of integral
coincides with that of Thm.
16

and Def.
17
. Note that (
2.12
) also directly
implies Fubini’s theorem for this type of integrals.

(vi)

Let
K\subseteq\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
be
\lambda
-measurable, where
\lambda

is the Lebesgue measure, and let
\varphi\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GC}^{\infty}(K,\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{d})
be
such that
\varphi^{-1}\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GC}^{\infty}(\varphi(K),\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{n})
. Then
\varphi(K)

is
\lambda
-measurable and

\int_{\varphi(K)}f\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\lambda}{\hbox{\rm d}\lambda}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\lambda$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\lambda$}}=\int_{K}(f\circ\varphi)\left|\det(\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\varphi}{\hbox{\rm d}\varphi}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\varphi$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\varphi$}})\right|\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\lambda}{\hbox{\rm d}\lambda}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\lambda$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\lambda$}}

for each
f\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GI}(\varphi(K),\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}})
.

In order to state a continuity property for this notion
of integration, we have to introduce
hypernatural numbers and
hyperlimits
as follows

Definition 34
.

(i)

\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}:=\left\{[n_{\varepsilon}]\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}\mid n_{\varepsilon}\in\mathbb{N}\ \forall\varepsilon\right\}
.
Elements of
\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}
are called
hypernatural numbers
or
hyperfinite
numbers
. We clearly have
\mathbb{N}\subseteq\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}
, but among hypernatural
numbers we also have infinite numbers.

(ii)

\mathbb{N}_{\rho}:=\left\{(n_{\varepsilon})\in\mathbb{R}_{\rho}\mid n_{\varepsilon}\in\mathbb{N}\ \forall\varepsilon\right\}
.

(iii)

A map
x:\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}\longrightarrow\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}
, whose
domain is the set of hyperfinite numbers
\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}
is called
a (
\sigma-
)
hypersequence
(of elements of
\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}
) and
denoted by
(x_{n})_{n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}}
, or simply
(x_{n})_{n}

if the gauge on the domain is clear from the context. Let
\sigma
,

\rho
be two gauges,
x:\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}\longrightarrow\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}
be a hypersequence
and
l\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}
. We say that
l
is the
hyperlimit
of
(x_{n})_{n}

as
n
\rightarrow\infty
and
n
\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}
, if

\forall q\in N\,\exists M\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}\,\forall n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}_{\geq M}:\ |x_{n}-l|<\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}^{q}.

It can be easily proved that there exists at most one hyperlimit,
and in this case it is denoted by
\mathchoice{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\raisebox{-0.39864pt}{$\rho\hskip-5.49028pt$}$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\raisebox{-0.39864pt}{$\rho\hskip-5.49028pt$}$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\raisebox{-0.39864pt}{$\rho\hskip-5.49028pt$}$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\raisebox{-0.39864pt}{$\rho\hskip-5.49028pt$}$}}}\lim_{n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}\,}}{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}\lim_{n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}\,}}{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}\lim_{n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}\,}}{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}\lim_{n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}\,}}x_{n}=l
.
Note that
\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}<\frac{1}{n}
if
n\in\mathbb{N}_{>0}
so that
\frac{1}{n}\not\to 0

in the sharp topology. On the contrary
\mathchoice{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\raisebox{-0.39864pt}{$\rho\hskip-5.49028pt$}$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\raisebox{-0.39864pt}{$\rho\hskip-5.49028pt$}$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\raisebox{-0.39864pt}{$\rho\hskip-5.49028pt$}$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\raisebox{-0.39864pt}{$\rho\hskip-5.49028pt$}$}}}\lim_{n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}\,}}{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}\lim_{n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}\,}}{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}\lim_{n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}\,}}{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}\lim_{n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}\,}}\frac{1}{n}=0

because
\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}
contains arbitrarily large infinite hypernatural
numbers.

The following continuity result once again underscores that functionally
compact sets (even if they can be unbounded from a classical point
of view) behaves as compact sets for GSF.

Theorem 35
.

Let
K\subseteq\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
be a
\mu
-measurable
functionally compact set and
f_{n}\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GC}^{\infty}(K,\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{d})
for all

n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}
. Then, if the hyperlimit
\mathchoice{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\raisebox{-0.42224pt}{$\rho\hskip-5.49028pt$}$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\raisebox{-0.42224pt}{$\rho\hskip-5.49028pt$}$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\raisebox{-0.42224pt}{$\rho\hskip-5.49028pt$}$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\raisebox{-0.42224pt}{$\rho\hskip-5.49028pt$}$}}}\lim_{n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}\,}}{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}\lim_{n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}\,}}{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}\lim_{n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}\,}}{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}\lim_{n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}\,}}f_{n}(x)

exists for each
x\in K
, then the convergence is uniform on
K

and
\mathchoice{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\raisebox{-0.42224pt}{$\rho\hskip-5.49028pt$}$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\raisebox{-0.42224pt}{$\rho\hskip-5.49028pt$}$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\raisebox{-0.42224pt}{$\rho\hskip-5.49028pt$}$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\raisebox{-0.42224pt}{$\rho\hskip-5.49028pt$}$}}}\lim_{n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}\,}}{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}\lim_{n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}\,}}{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}\lim_{n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}\,}}{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}\lim_{n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}\,}}f_{n}\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GC}^{\infty}(K,\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{d})
. Finally

\mathchoice{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\raisebox{-0.42224pt}{$\rho\hskip-5.49028pt$}$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\raisebox{-0.42224pt}{$\rho\hskip-5.49028pt$}$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\raisebox{-0.42224pt}{$\rho\hskip-5.49028pt$}$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\raisebox{-0.42224pt}{$\rho\hskip-5.49028pt$}$}}}\lim_{n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}\,}}{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}\lim_{n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}\,}}{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}\lim_{n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}\,}}{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}\lim_{n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}\,}}\int_{K}f_{n}\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}x_{n}}{\hbox{\rm d}x_{n}}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}x_{n}$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}x_{n}$}}=\int_{K}\mathchoice{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\raisebox{-0.42224pt}{$\rho\hskip-5.49028pt$}$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\raisebox{-0.42224pt}{$\rho\hskip-5.49028pt$}$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\raisebox{-0.42224pt}{$\rho\hskip-5.49028pt$}$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\raisebox{-0.42224pt}{$\rho\hskip-5.49028pt$}$}}}\lim_{n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}\,}}{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}\lim_{n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}\,}}{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}\lim_{n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}\,}}{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}\lim_{n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}\,}}f_{n}\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}x_{n}}{\hbox{\rm d}x_{n}}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}x_{n}$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}x_{n}$}}.

(2.13)

For the proof of this theorem see
[
27
]
, and for
the notion of hyperlimit see
[
47
]
.

3.
Convolution on
\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{n}

In this section, we define and study convolution
f*g
of two GSF,
where
f
or
g
is compactly supported. Compactly supported GSF
were introduced in
[
24
]
for the gauge
\rho_{\varepsilon}=\varepsilon
.
For an arbitrary gauge, we here define and study the notions needed
for the HFT as well as for the study of convolution of GSF.

Definition 36
.

Assume that
X\subseteq\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
,
Y\subseteq\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
and
f\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GC}^{\infty}\left(X,Y\right)
,
then

(i)

\text{supp}\left(f\right):=\overline{\left\{x\in X\mid\left|f\left(x\right)\right|>0\right\}}
,
where
\overline{\left(\cdot\right)}
denotes the relative closure
in
X
with respect to the sharp topology, is called the
support

of
f
. We recall (see just after Def.
1
and Lem.
4
)
that
x>0
means that
x\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}_{\geq 0}
is positive and invertible.

(ii)

For
A\subseteq\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}
we call the set
\text{ext}\left(A\right):=\left\{x\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}\mid\forall a\in A:\ \left|x-a\right|>0\right\}

the
strong exterior of
A
. Recalling Lem.
4
,
if
x\in\text{ext}(A)
, then
|x-a|\geq\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}^{q}
for all
a\in A

and for some
q=q(a)\in\mathbb{N}
.

(iii)

Let
H\Subset_{\text{\rm f}}\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
, we say that
f\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{D}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{D}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{D}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{D}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GD}\left(H,Y\right)

if
f\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GC}^{\infty}(\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{n},Y)
and
\text{supp}\left(f\right)\subseteq H
.
We say that
f\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{D}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{D}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{D}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{D}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GD}(\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{n},Y)
if
f\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{D}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{D}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{D}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{D}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GD}\left(H,Y\right)

for some
H\Subset_{\text{\rm f}}\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
. Such an
f
is called
compactly
supported
; for simplicity we set
\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{D}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{D}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{D}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{D}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GD}(H):=\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{D}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{D}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{D}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{D}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GD}(H,\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{C}})
. Note
that
\text{supp}(f)
is clearly always closed, and if
f\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{D}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{D}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{D}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{D}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GD}\left(H,Y\right)

then it is also sharply bounded. However, in general it is not an
internal set so it is not a functionally compact set. Accordingly,
the theory of multidimensional integration of Sec.
2.7.2

does not allow us to consider
\int_{\text{supp}(f)}f
even if
f

is compactly supported.

Remark 37
.

(i)

Note that the notion of
standard support

\text{stsupp}\left(f\right)

as defined in Thm.
22
and the present notion
\text{supp}\left(f\right)

of support, as defined above, are different. The main distinction
is that
\text{stsupp}\left(f\right)\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{n}
while

\text{supp}\left(f\right)\subseteq\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
. Moreover if we consider
a CGF
f\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GC}^{\infty}({\text{\rm c}}({\Omega}),\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{d})
, then
\text{supp}\left(f\right)\cap\Omega\subseteq\text{stsupp}\left(f\right)
.

(ii)

Since
\delta\left(0\right)>0
then
\delta|_{B_{r}\left(0\right)}>0

for some
r\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}
by the sharp continuity of
\delta
,
i.e. Thm.
13
.
(ii)
, hence
B_{r}\left(0\right)\subseteq\text{supp}\left(\delta\right)
,
whereas
\text{stsupp}\left(\delta\right)=\left\{0\right\}
. Example

24
.
(i)
also yields that
\text{supp}(\delta)\subseteq[-r,r]^{n}

for all
r\in\mathbb{R}_{>0}
.

(iii)

Any rapidly decreasing function
f\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{n})

satisfies the inequality
0\leq f\left(x\right)\leq\left|x\right|^{-q}
,

\forall q\in\mathbb{N}
, for
\left|x\right|
finite sufficiently
large. Therefore, for all strongly infinite
x
, we have
f\left(x\right)=0

i.e.,
f\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{D}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{D}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{D}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{D}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GD}\left(\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{n}\right)
.

Lemma 38
.

Let
\emptyset\neq H\Subset_{f}\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
. Then

\text{\emph{ext}}\left(H\right)
is sharply open.

Proof.

If
x=\left[x_{\varepsilon}\right]\in\text{ext}\left(H\right)
, we set
d_{\varepsilon}:=d\left(x_{\varepsilon},H_{\varepsilon}\right)

where
H=\left[H_{\varepsilon}\right]
and
\emptyset\neq H_{\varepsilon}\Subset\mathbb{R}^{n}

for all
\varepsilon
(because
H\neq\emptyset
). Then
\exists h_{\varepsilon}\in H_{\varepsilon}:\ d:=d\left(x_{\varepsilon},h_{\varepsilon}\right)
,
we set
h:=\left[h_{\varepsilon}\right]\in H
and
\left|x-h\right|=\left[d_{\varepsilon}\right]=:d>0

because
x\in\text{ext}(H)
and
h\in H
. Now, by taking
r:=\frac{d}{2}>0
,
we prove that
B_{r}\left(x\right)\subseteq\text{ext}\left(H\right)
.
Pick
y\in B_{r}\left(x\right)
, then for all
a\in H
, we have

\left|y-a\right|\geq|x-a|-|y-x|\geq d-\frac{d}{2}>0
.
∎

Theorem 39
.

Let
H\Subset_{\text{\rm f}}\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
and
f\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GC}^{\infty}(\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{n},\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{C}})
,
then the following properties hold:

(i)

f\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{D}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{D}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{D}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{D}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GD}\left(H\right)
if and only if

f|_{\text{\emph{ext}}(H)}=0
.

If
f\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{D}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{D}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{D}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{D}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GD}\left(H\right)
,
x\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
and
\alpha\in\mathbb{N}^{n}
,
then:

(ii)

\partial^{\alpha}f\left(x\right)=0
for all

x\in\text{\emph{ext}}(H)
.

(iii)

If
H\subseteq[-h,h]^{n}
then
\partial^{\alpha}f(x)=0

whenever
x_{p}\geq h
or
x_{p}\leq-h
for some
p=1,\ldots,n
.

(iv)

If
H\subseteq[-h,h]^{n}\subseteq\prod_{p=1}^{n}[a_{p},b_{p}]
,
then

\intop_{a_{1}}^{b_{1}}\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}x_{1}}{\hbox{\rm d}x_{1}}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}x_{1}$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}x_{1}$}}\ldots\intop_{a_{n}}^{b_{n}}f\left(x\right)\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}x_{n}}{\hbox{\rm d}x_{n}}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}x_{n}$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}x_{n}$}}=\intop_{-h}^{h}\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}x_{1}}{\hbox{\rm d}x_{1}}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}x_{1}$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}x_{1}$}}\ldots\intop_{-h}^{h}f\left(x\right)\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}x_{n}}{\hbox{\rm d}x_{n}}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}x_{n}$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}x_{n}$}}

Proof.

(i)
: Assume that
\text{supp}(f)\subseteq H

and
x=[x_{\varepsilon}]\in\text{ext}(H)
, but
f(x)\neq 0
. This implies
that
|f(x)|\not\leq 0
because always
|f(x)|\geq 0
. Thereby, Lem.
8

yields
|f(x)|>_{L}0
for some
L\subseteq_{0}I
. Applying Lem.
4

for the ring
\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}|_{L}
we get
|f(x)|>_{L}\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}^{q}
for
some
q\in\mathbb{R}_{>0}
, i.e.
\left|f_{\varepsilon}(x_{\varepsilon})\right|>\rho_{\varepsilon}^{q}

for all
\varepsilon\in L_{\leq\varepsilon_{0}}
. Define
\bar{x}_{\varepsilon}:=x_{\varepsilon}

for all
\varepsilon\in L
and
\bar{x}_{\varepsilon}:=x_{\varepsilon_{0}}
otherwise,
so that
\bar{x}:=[\bar{x}_{\varepsilon}]\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
and
|f(\bar{x})|>\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}^{q}
.
This yields
\bar{x}\in\text{supp}(f)\subseteq H
, and hence
|x-\bar{x}|>0
,
which is impossible by construction because
\bar{x}|_{L}=x|_{L}

and because of Lem.
4
.

Vice versa, assume that
f|_{\text{ext}(H)}=0
and take
x=[x_{\varepsilon}]\in\text{supp}(f)\setminus H
.
The property

\forall q\in\mathbb{R}_{>0}\,\forall^{0}\varepsilon:\ d(x_{\varepsilon},H_{\varepsilon})\leq\rho_{\varepsilon}^{q}

cannot hold, because for
q\to+\infty
Thm.
11
.
(i)

would imply
x\in H=[H_{\varepsilon}]
. Therefore, for some
q\in\mathbb{R}_{>0}

and some
L\subseteq_{0}I
, we have
d(x_{\varepsilon},H_{\varepsilon})\geq\rho_{\varepsilon}^{q}

for all
\varepsilon\in L
. Thereby, if
a=[a_{\varepsilon}]\in H
where
a_{\varepsilon}\in H_{\varepsilon}

for all
\varepsilon
, we get
d(x_{\varepsilon},a_{\varepsilon})\geq d(x_{\varepsilon},H_{\varepsilon})\geq\rho_{\varepsilon}^{q}

for all
\varepsilon\in L
, i.e.
x|_{L}\in\text{ext}(H)|_{L}
. Applying
Lem.
38
for the ring
\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}|_{L}
we get

B_{r}(x)|_{L}\subseteq\text{ext}(H)|_{L}

(3.1)

for some
r\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}
. From
x\in\text{supp}(f)
, we get the
existence of a sequence
(x_{p})_{p\in\mathbb{N}}
of points of
\left\{x\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{n}\mid|f(x)|>0\right\}

such that
x_{p}\to x
as
p\to+\infty
in the sharp topology. Therefore,

x_{p}\in B_{r}(x)
for
p\in\mathbb{N}
sufficiently large. Thereby,
x_{p}|_{L}\in\text{ext}(H)|_{L}

from (
3.1
) and hence
f(x_{p})|_{L}=\left[\left(f_{\varepsilon}(x_{p\varepsilon})\right)_{\varepsilon\in L}\right]=0
,
which contradicts
|f(x_{p})|>0
.

Property
(ii)
follows by induction on
|\alpha|\in\mathbb{N}

using Thm.
14
. We prove property
(iii)

for the case
x_{p}\geq h
, the other case being similar. We consider

\bar{x}_{q}:=(x_{1},\displaystyle\mathop{\ldots\ldots\,}^{p-1},x_{p-1},x_{p}+\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}^{q},x_{p+1},\ldots,x_{n})\quad\forall q\in\mathbb{N}.

Then
|\bar{x}_{q}-a|\geq|x_{p}+\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}^{q}-a_{p}|\geq\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}^{q}

for all
a\in[-h,h]^{n}\supseteq H
because
x_{p}\geq h\geq a_{p}
.
Therefore,
\bar{x}_{q}\in\text{ext}(H)
and hence
\partial^{\alpha}f(\bar{x}_{q})=0

from the previous
(ii)
. The conclusion now follows
from the sharp continuity of the GSF
\partial^{\alpha}f
(Thm.
13
.
(ii)
).

(iv)
: The inclusion
\pm(h,\ldots,h)\in[-h,h]^{n}\subseteq\prod_{p=1}^{n}[a_{p},b_{p}]

implies
a_{p}\leq-h
and
b_{p}\geq h
for all
p=1,\ldots,n
. Using
Thm.
33
.
(v)
, we
can write

\displaystyle\intop_{a_{1}}^{b_{1}}\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}x_{1}}{\hbox{\rm d}x_{1}}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}x_{1}$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}x_{1}$}}\ldots\intop_{a_{n}}^{b_{n}}f\left(x\right)\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}x_{n}}{\hbox{\rm d}x_{n}}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}x_{n}$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}x_{n}$}}

\displaystyle=\intop_{a_{1}}^{b_{1}}\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}x_{1}}{\hbox{\rm d}x_{1}}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}x_{1}$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}x_{1}$}}\ldots\intop_{a_{n-1}}^{b_{n-1}}\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}x_{n-1}}{\hbox{\rm d}x_{n-1}}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}x_{n-1}$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}x_{n-1}$}}\intop_{a_{n}}^{-h}f\left(x\right)\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}x_{n}}{\hbox{\rm d}x_{n}}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}x_{n}$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}x_{n}$}}+

\displaystyle\phantom{=}\intop_{a_{1}}^{b_{1}}\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}x_{1}}{\hbox{\rm d}x_{1}}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}x_{1}$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}x_{1}$}}\ldots\intop_{a_{n-1}}^{b_{n-1}}\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}x_{n-1}}{\hbox{\rm d}x_{n-1}}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}x_{n-1}$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}x_{n-1}$}}\intop_{-h}^{+h}f\left(x\right)\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}x_{n}}{\hbox{\rm d}x_{n}}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}x_{n}$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}x_{n}$}}+

\displaystyle\phantom{=}\intop_{a_{1}}^{b_{1}}\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}x_{1}}{\hbox{\rm d}x_{1}}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}x_{1}$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}x_{1}$}}\ldots\intop_{a_{n-1}}^{b_{n-1}}\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}x_{n-1}}{\hbox{\rm d}x_{n-1}}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}x_{n-1}$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}x_{n-1}$}}\intop_{h}^{b_{n}}f\left(x\right)\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}x_{n}}{\hbox{\rm d}x_{n}}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}x_{n}$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}x_{n}$}}.

But if
x_{n}\in[a_{n},-h]
or
x_{n}\in[h,b_{n}]
, then property

(iii)
yields
f(x)=0
and we obtain

\intop_{a_{1}}^{b_{1}}\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}x_{1}}{\hbox{\rm d}x_{1}}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}x_{1}$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}x_{1}$}}\ldots\intop_{a_{n}}^{b_{n}}f\left(x\right)\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}x_{n}}{\hbox{\rm d}x_{n}}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}x_{n}$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}x_{n}$}}=\intop_{a_{1}}^{b_{1}}\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}x_{1}}{\hbox{\rm d}x_{1}}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}x_{1}$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}x_{1}$}}\ldots\intop_{a_{n-1}}^{b_{n-1}}\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}x_{n-1}}{\hbox{\rm d}x_{n-1}}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}x_{n-1}$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}x_{n-1}$}}\intop_{-h}^{h}f\left(x\right)\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}x_{n}}{\hbox{\rm d}x_{n}}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}x_{n}$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}x_{n}$}}.

Proceeding in the same way with all the other integrals we get the
claim.
∎

In particular, if
T\in\mathcal{E}^{\prime}(\Omega)
, then Thm.
39
.
(i)

implies that
\iota_{\Omega}^{b}(T)\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{D}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{D}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{D}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{D}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GD}\left(\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{n}\right)
.
Also observe that
f(x)=e^{-x^{2}}
,
x\in\left\{x\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}\mid\exists N\in\mathbb{N}:\ x^{2}\geq N\log\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}\right\}
,
satisfies
f(x)\leq x^{-q}
for all infinite
x
and all
q\in\mathbb{N}
.
Therefore

\forall Q\in\mathbb{N}:\ f\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{D}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{D}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{D}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{D}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GD}\left([-\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}^{-Q},\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}^{-Q}]\right).

Based on these results, we can define

Definition 40
.

Let
f\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{D}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{D}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{D}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{D}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GD}(\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{n})
, then

\int f:=\int_{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{n}}f:=\intop_{a_{1}}^{b_{1}}\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}x_{1}}{\hbox{\rm d}x_{1}}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}x_{1}$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}x_{1}$}}\ldots\intop_{a_{n}}^{b_{n}}f\left(x\right)\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}x_{n}}{\hbox{\rm d}x_{n}}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}x_{n}$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}x_{n}$}}

(3.2)

where
\text{supp}(f)\subseteq\prod_{p=1}^{n}[a_{p},b_{p}]
. This
equality does not depend on
a_{p}
,
b_{p}
because of Thm.
39
.
(iv)
.

Note that we can also write (
3.2
) as

\int f=\lim_{\begin{subarray}{c}a_{p}\to-\infty\\
b_{p}\to+\infty\\
p=1,\ldots,n\end{subarray}}\ \intop_{a_{1}}^{b_{1}}\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}x_{1}}{\hbox{\rm d}x_{1}}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}x_{1}$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}x_{1}$}}\ldots\intop_{a_{n}}^{b_{n}}f\left(x\right)\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}x_{n}}{\hbox{\rm d}x_{n}}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}x_{n}$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}x_{n}$}}=\lim_{h\to+\infty}\intop_{-h}^{h}\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}x_{1}}{\hbox{\rm d}x_{1}}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}x_{1}$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}x_{1}$}}\ldots\intop_{-h}^{h}f\left(x\right)\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}x_{n}}{\hbox{\rm d}x_{n}}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}x_{n}$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}x_{n}$}}

(3.3)

even if we are actually considering limits of eventually constant
functions. Using this notion of integral of a compactly supported
GSF, we can also write the value of a distribution
\langle T,\varphi\rangle

as an integral: let
b\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}
be a strong infinite number,

\Omega\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{n}
be an open set,
T\in\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(\Omega)

and
\varphi\in\mathcal{D}(\Omega
), with
\text{supp}(\varphi)\subseteq\prod_{i=1}^{n}[a_{i},b_{i}]_{\mathbb{R}}=:J
.
Then from Thm.
22
.
(iii)
and Thm.
33
.
(v)

we get

\langle T,\varphi\rangle=\int_{[J]}\iota_{\Omega}^{b}(T)(x)\cdot\varphi(x)\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}x}{\hbox{\rm d}x}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}x$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}x$}}=\int\iota_{\Omega}^{b}(T)(x)\cdot\varphi(x)\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}x}{\hbox{\rm d}x}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}x$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}x$}},

(3.4)

where the equalities are in
\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}
.

Definition 41
.

Let
f
,
g\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GC}^{\infty}(\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{n})
, with
f\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{D}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{D}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{D}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{D}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GD}(\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{n})

or
g\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{D}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{D}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{D}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{D}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GD}(\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{n})
. In the former case, by Thm.
13
.
(iv)

and Thm.
39
.
(i)
, for
all
x\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
,
f\cdot g(x-\cdot)\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{D}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{D}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{D}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{D}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GD}(\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{n})
with
\text{supp}\left(f\cdot g(x-\cdot)\right)\subseteq\text{supp}(f)\Subset_{\text{\rm f}}\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
.
Moreover,
\text{supp}\left(f(x-\cdot)\cdot g\right)\subseteq x-\text{supp}(f)\Subset_{\text{\rm f}}\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
.
Similarly, we can argue in the latter case, and we can hence define

\left(f\ast g\right)\left(x\right):=\intop f\left(y\right)g\left(x-y\right)\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}y}{\hbox{\rm d}y}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}y$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}y$}}=\intop f\left(x-y\right)g\left(y\right)\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}y}{\hbox{\rm d}y}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}y$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}y$}}\quad\forall x\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{n}.

(3.5)

Note that directly from Thm.
16

and Def.
40
, it follows that
f*g\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GC}^{\infty}(\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{n})
.
The next theorems provide the usual basic properties of convolution
suitably formulated in our framework. We start by studying how the
convolution is in relation to the supports of its factors:

Theorem 42
.

Let
f
,
g
,
h\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{D}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{D}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{D}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{D}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GD}(\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{n})
. Then
the following properties hold:

(i)

Let
\text{\emph{supp}}(f)\subseteq[-a,a]^{n}
,

\text{\emph{supp}}(g)\subseteq[-b,b]^{n}
,
a
,
b\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}
,
and
x\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
. Set
L_{x}:=[-a,a]^{n}\cap\left(x-[-b,b]^{n}\right)
,
then

\displaystyle\text{\emph{supp}}\left(f\cdot g(x-\cdot)\right)

\displaystyle\subseteq L_{x}=\prod_{p=1}^{n}[\max(-a,x_{p}-b),\min(a,x_{p}+b)]

(3.6)

\displaystyle\left(f\ast g\right)\left(x\right)

\displaystyle=\intop_{L_{x}}f\left(y\right)g\left(x-y\right)\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}y}{\hbox{\rm d}y}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}y$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}y$}}.

(3.7)

(ii)

\text{\emph{supp}}(f*g)\subseteq\overline{\text{\emph{supp}}(f)+\text{\emph{supp}}(g)}
,
therefore
