f*g\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{D}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{D}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{D}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{D}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GD}(\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{n})
.

Proof.

(i)
: If
|f(t)g(x-t)|>0
, then
t\in\text{supp}(f)

and
x-t\in\text{supp}(g)
. Therefore,
\text{supp}\left(f\cdot g(x-\cdot)\right)\subseteq[-a,a]^{n}\cap\left(x-[-b,b]^{n}\right)
.
As in the case of real numbers, we can say that if
t\in[-a,a]^{n}\cap\left(x-[-b,b]^{n}\right)
,
then
-a\leq t_{p}\leq a
and
-b\leq x_{p}-t_{p}\leq b
for all
p=1,\ldots,n
.
Therefore,
t_{p}\in[\max(-a,x_{p}-b),\min(a,x_{p}+b)]
. Similarly,
we can prove that also
L_{x}\subseteq[-a,a]^{n}\cap\left(x-[-b,b]^{n}\right)
.
The conclusion (
3.6
) now follows from Def.
40
.
For completeness, recall that in general
\text{supp}(f)
and
\text{supp}(g)

are not functionally compact sets and our integration theory allows
to integrate only over the latter kind of sets. This justifies our
formulation of the present property using intervals.

(ii)
: Since
f
and
g
are compactly supported, we
have
\text{supp}(f)\subseteq H
and
\text{supp}(g)\subseteq L

for some
H
,
L\Subset_{\text{\rm f}}\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
. Assume that
\left|(f*g)(x)\right|>0
.
Then, by Thm.
18
.
(vii)
, Thm.
33
.
(v)

and the extreme value Thm.
25
, we get

0<\left|(f*g)(x)\right|\leq\lambda(H)\cdot\max_{y\in H}|f(y)g(x-y)|,

where
\lambda
is the extension of the Lebesgue measure given by
Def.
32
. Therefore, there exists
y\in H

such that
0<\lambda(H)\cdot\left|f(y)g(x-y)\right|
. This implies
that
y\in\text{supp}(f)
and
x-y\in\text{supp}(g)
. Thereby,
x=y+(x-y)\in\text{supp}(f)+\text{supp}(g)
.
Taking the sharp closure we get the conclusion. Finally,
\overline{\text{supp}(f)+\text{supp}(g)}\subseteq\overline{H+L}=H+L

and
H+L\Subset_{\text{\rm f}}\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
because it is the image under the sum
+

of
H\times L
(see Thm.
30
and Thm.
28
).
∎

Now, we consider algebraic properties of convolution and its relations
with derivations and integration:

Theorem 43
.

Let
f
,
g
,
h\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GC}^{\infty}(\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{n})
and
assume that at least two of them are compactly supported. Then the
following properties hold:

(i)

f\ast g=g*f
.

(ii)

\left(f\ast g\right)\ast h=f\ast\left(g\ast h\right)
.

(iii)

f\ast\left(h+g\right)=f\ast h+f\ast g
.

(iv)

\overline{f*g}=\overline{f}*\overline{g}

(v)

{\displaystyle t\oplus\left(f*g\right)=\left(t\oplus f\right)*g=f*\left(t\oplus g\right)}

where
t\oplus f
is the translation of the function
f
by
t

defined by
\left(t\oplus f\right)\left(x\right)=f\left(x-t\right)

(see Sec.
2.6
).

(vi)

\frac{\partial}{\partial x_{p}}\left(f*g\right)=\frac{\partial f}{\partial x_{p}}*g=f*\frac{\partial g}{\partial x_{p}}

for all
p=1,\ldots,n
.

(vii)

If both
f
and
g
are compactly supported,
then

\intop\left(f\ast g\right)\left(x\right)\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}x}{\hbox{\rm d}x}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}x$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}x$}}=\left(\intop f\left(x\right)\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}x}{\hbox{\rm d}x}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}x$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}x$}}\right)\left(\intop g\left(x\right)\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}x}{\hbox{\rm d}x}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}x$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}x$}}\right).

Proof.

(i)
: We assume, e.g., that
f\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{D}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{D}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{D}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{D}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GD}(\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{n})
.
Take
h\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}
such that
\text{supp}(f)\subseteq[-h,h]^{n}
.
By (
3.7
) and Def.
40
, we can write

\left(f\ast g\right)\left(x\right)=\intop_{-h}^{h}\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}y_{1}}{\hbox{\rm d}y_{1}}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}y_{1}$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}y_{1}$}}\ldots\intop_{-h}^{h}f\left(y\right)g\left(x-y\right)\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}y_{n}}{\hbox{\rm d}y_{n}}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}y_{n}$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}y_{n}$}}.

We can now proceed as in the classical case, i.e. considering the
change of variable
z=x-y
(Thm.
19
).
We get

\left(f\ast g\right)\left(x\right)=\intop_{x_{1}-h}^{x_{1}+h}\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}z_{1}}{\hbox{\rm d}z_{1}}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}z_{1}$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}z_{1}$}}\ldots\intop_{x_{n}-h}^{x_{n}+h}f\left(x-z\right)g\left(z\right)\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}z_{n}}{\hbox{\rm d}z_{n}}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}z_{n}$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}z_{n}$}}.

Taking the limit
h\to+\infty
(see (
3.3
)),
we obtain the desired equality. Similarly, we can also prove
(ii)

and
(iii)
.

As usual,
(iv)
is a straightforward consequence
of the definition of complex conjugate.

(v)
: The usual proof applies, in fact

\displaystyle t\oplus\left(f*g\right)\left(x\right)

\displaystyle=\left(f*g\right)\left(x-t\right)=\intop f\left(y\right)g\left(x-t-y\right)\,\mathrm{d}y=

\displaystyle=\intop f\left(y\right)\left(t\oplus g\right)\left(x-y\right)\,\mathrm{d}y=\left(f\ast\left(t\oplus g\right)\right)\left(x\right).

(3.8)

Finally, the commutativity property
(i)
yields

\left(t\oplus f\right)*g=g*(t\oplus f)
and applying (
3.8
)

g*(t\oplus f)=t\oplus\left(g*f\right)=t\oplus\left(f*g\right)
.

(vi)
: Set
h:=f*g
and take
x\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
.
Using differentiation under the integral sign (Thm.
18
.
(viii)
)
and Def.
40
we get

\frac{\partial}{\partial x_{p}}h\left(x\right)=\intop_{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{n}}f\left(y\right)\frac{\partial g}{\partial x_{p}}\left(x-y\right)\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}y}{\hbox{\rm d}y}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}y$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}y$}}=\left(f\ast\frac{\partial g}{\partial x_{p}}\right)\left(x\right).

Using
(i)
, we also have
\frac{\partial}{\partial x_{p}}h=\frac{\partial f}{\partial x_{p}}\ast g
.

To prove
(vii)
we show the case
n=1
, even
if the general one is similar. Let
a
,
b\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}
be such
that
\text{supp}(f*g)\subseteq[-a,a]
(Thm.
42
)
and
\text{supp}(f)\subseteq[-b,b]
. Then

\int(f*g)(x)\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}x}{\hbox{\rm d}x}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}x$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}x$}}=\int_{-a}^{a}\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}x}{\hbox{\rm d}x}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}x$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}x$}}\int_{-b}^{b}f(y)g(x-y)\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}y}{\hbox{\rm d}y}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}y$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}y$}}.

Using Fubini’s Thm.
33
.
(v)
,
we can write

\displaystyle\int(f*g)(x)\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}x}{\hbox{\rm d}x}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}x$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}x$}}

\displaystyle=\int_{-b}^{b}f(y)\int_{-a}^{a}g(x-y)\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}x}{\hbox{\rm d}x}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}x$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}x$}}\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}y}{\hbox{\rm d}y}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}y$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}y$}}=

\displaystyle=\int_{-b}^{b}f(y)\int_{-a-y}^{a-y}g(z)\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}z}{\hbox{\rm d}z}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}z$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}z$}}\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}y}{\hbox{\rm d}y}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}y$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}y$}}=

\displaystyle=\int_{-b}^{b}f(y)\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}y}{\hbox{\rm d}y}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}y$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}y$}}\int_{-c}^{c}g(z)\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}z}{\hbox{\rm d}z}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}z$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}z$}},

where we have taken
a\to+\infty
or equivalently, considered any

c\geq a+b
.
∎

Young’s inequality for convolution is based on the generalized Hölder’s
inequality, on the inequality
\left|\int_{K}f\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\mu}{\hbox{\rm d}\mu}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\mu$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\mu$}}\right|\leq\int_{K}\left|f\right|\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\mu}{\hbox{\rm d}\mu}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\mu$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\mu$}}

(see Thm.
33
.
(iv)
),
monotonicity of integral (see Thm.
18
.
(vii)
)
and Fubini’s theorem (see Thm.
33
.
(v)
).
Therefore, the usual proofs can be repeated in our setting if we take
sufficient care of terms such as
|f(x)|^{p}
if
p\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}_{\geq 1}
:

Definition 44
.

Let
f\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{D}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{D}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{D}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{D}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GD}(\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{n})
and
p\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}_{\geq 1}

be a finite number. Then, we set

\|f\|_{p}:=\left(\int|f(x)|^{p}\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}x}{\hbox{\rm d}x}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}x$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}x$}}\right)^{1/p}\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}_{\geq 0}.

Note that
|f|^{p}
is a generalized integrable function (Def.
32
)
because
p
is a finite number (in general the power
x^{y}
is
not well-defined, e.g.
\left(\frac{1}{\rho_{\varepsilon}}\right)^{1/\rho_{\varepsilon}}=\rho_{\varepsilon}^{-1/\rho_{\varepsilon}}

is not
\rho
-moderate).

On the other hand, Hölder’s inequality, if
\|f\|_{p}>0

and
\|g\|_{q}>0
, is simply based on monotonicity of integral,
Fubini’s theorem and Young’s inequality for products. The latter holds
also in
\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}_{\geq 0}
because it holds in the entire
\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
,
see e.g.
[
57
]
.

Theorem 45

(Hölder)
.

Let
f_{k}\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{D}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{D}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{D}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{D}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GD}(\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{n})
and
p_{k}\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}_{\geq 1}

for all
k=1,\ldots,m
be such that
\sum_{k=1}^{m}\frac{1}{p_{k}}=1

and
\|f_{k}\|_{p_{k}}>0
. Then

\left\|\prod_{k=1}^{m}f_{k}\right\|_{1}\leq\prod_{k=1}^{m}\|f_{k}\|_{p_{k}}.

Theorem 46

(Young)
.

Let
f
,
g\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{D}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{D}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{D}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{D}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GD}(\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{n})
and
p
,

q
,
r\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}_{\geq 1}
be such that
{\displaystyle{\textstyle{\frac{1}{p}}+{\frac{1}{q}}=1+{\frac{1}{r}}}}

and
\|f\|_{p}
,
\|g\|_{q}>0
, then
\|f*g\|_{r}\leq\|f\|_{p}\cdot\|g\|_{q}
.

In the following theorem, we consider when the equality

\left(\delta*f\right)(x)=f(x)
holds. As we will see later in Sec.
5
,
as a consequence of the Riemann-Lebesgue lemma we necessarily have
a limitation concerning the validity of this equality.

Theorem 47
.

Let
\delta
be the
\iota_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}^{b}
-embedding
of the
n
-dimensional Dirac delta (see Thm.
22
).
Assume that
f\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GC}^{\infty}\left(\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{n}\right)
satisfies, at the point

x\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
, the condition

\exists r\in\mathbb{R}_{>0}\,\exists M,c\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}\,\forall y\in\overline{B}_{r}(x)\,\forall j\in\mathbb{N}:\ \left|\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}}{\hbox{\rm d}}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}$}}^{j}f\left(y\right)\right|\leq Mc^{j},

(3.9)

\frac{b}{c}\text{ is a large infinite number}

i.e. in a
finite
neighborhood of
x
all its differentials

\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}^{j}}{\hbox{\rm d}^{j}}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}^{j}$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}^{j}$}}f(y)
are bounded by a suitably small polynomial
Mc^{j}

(such a function
f
will be called
bounded by a tame polynomial
at

x
). Then
\left(\delta\ast f\right)(x)=f(x)
.

Proof.

Considering that
\delta(y)=b^{n}\psi(by)
, where
\psi
is the
considered
n
-dimensional Colombeau mollifier and
b
is a strong
infinite number. (see Example
24
.
(i)
),
we have:

\displaystyle\left(\delta\ast f\right)\left(x\right)-f\left(x\right)

\displaystyle=\int f\left(x-y\right)\delta\left(y\right)\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}y}{\hbox{\rm d}y}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}y$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}y$}}-f\left(x\right)\int\delta\left(y\right)\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}y}{\hbox{\rm d}y}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}y$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}y$}}=

\displaystyle=\int\left(f\left(x-y\right)-f\left(x\right)\right)\delta\left(y\right)\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}y}{\hbox{\rm d}y}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}y$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}y$}}=

\displaystyle=\int_{\left[-\frac{r}{\sqrt{n}},\frac{r}{\sqrt{n}}\right]^{n}}\left(f\left(x-y\right)-f\left(x\right)\right)\delta\left(y\right)\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}y}{\hbox{\rm d}y}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}y$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}y$}}=

\displaystyle=\int_{\left[-\frac{r}{\sqrt{n}},\frac{r}{\sqrt{n}}\right]^{n}}\left(f\left(x-y\right)-f\left(x\right)\right)b^{n}\psi\left(by\right)\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}y}{\hbox{\rm d}y}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}y$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}y$}},

where
r\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}
is the radius from (
3.9
), so
that
\text{supp}(\delta)\subseteq\left[-\frac{r}{\sqrt{n}},\frac{r}{\sqrt{n}}\right]^{n}

since
r\in\mathbb{R}_{>0}
. By changing the variable
by=t
, and setting

H:=\left[-\frac{br}{\sqrt{n}},\frac{br}{\sqrt{n}}\right]^{n}
we
have

\left(f\ast\delta\right)\left(x\right)-f\left(x\right)=\int_{H}\left(f\left(x-\frac{t}{b}\right)-f\left(x\right)\right)\psi\left(t\right)\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}t}{\hbox{\rm d}t}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}t$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}t$}}.

Using Taylor’s formula (Thm.
20
.
(ii)
)
up to an arbitrary order
q\in\mathbb{N}
, we get

\int_{H}\left(f\left(x-\frac{t}{b}\right)-f\left(x\right)\right)\psi\left(t\right)\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}t}{\hbox{\rm d}t}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}t$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}t$}}=\int_{H}\sum_{0<\left|\alpha\right|\leq q}\frac{1}{\alpha!}\left(-\frac{t}{b}\right)^{\alpha}\partial^{\alpha}f\left(x\right)\psi\left(t\right)\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}t}{\hbox{\rm d}t}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}t$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}t$}}+\\
+\int_{H}\frac{1}{(q+1)!}\int_{0}^{1}\left(1-z\right)^{q}\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}^{q+1}f}{\hbox{\rm d}^{q+1}f}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}^{q+1}f$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}^{q+1}f$}}\left(x-z\frac{t}{b}\right)\left(-\frac{t}{b}\right)^{q+1}\psi\left(t\right)\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}z}{\hbox{\rm d}z}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}z$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}z$}}\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}t}{\hbox{\rm d}t}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}t$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}t$}}.

(3.10)

But
(i)
and
(v)

of Lem.
21
yield:

\int_{H}t^{\alpha}\psi(t)\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}t}{\hbox{\rm d}t}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}t$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}t$}}=\left[\int_{\left[-\frac{b_{\varepsilon}r}{\sqrt{n}},\frac{b_{\varepsilon}r}{\sqrt{n}}\right]^{n}}t^{\alpha}\psi_{\varepsilon}(t)\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}t}{\hbox{\rm d}t}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}t$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}t$}}\right]=\left[\int t^{\alpha}\psi_{\varepsilon}(t)\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}t}{\hbox{\rm d}t}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}t$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}t$}}\right]=0\quad\forall|\alpha|\leq q,

where we also used that
\frac{b_{\varepsilon}r}{\sqrt{n}}>1
for
\varepsilon

sufficiently small because
b>0
is an infinite number and
r\in\mathbb{R}_{>0}
.
Thereby, in (
3.10
) we only have to consider the remainder

R_{q}\left(x\right):=\int_{H}\frac{1}{(q+1)!}\int_{0}^{1}\left(1-z\right)^{q}\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}^{q+1}f}{\hbox{\rm d}^{q+1}f}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}^{q+1}f$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}^{q+1}f$}}\left(x-z\frac{t}{b}\right)\left(-\frac{t}{b}\right)^{q+1}\psi\left(t\right)\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}z}{\hbox{\rm d}z}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}z$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}z$}}\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}t}{\hbox{\rm d}t}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}t$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}t$}}=\\
=\frac{(-1)^{q+1}}{b^{q+1}(q+1)!}\int_{H}\int_{0}^{1}\left(1-z\right)^{q}\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}^{q+1}f}{\hbox{\rm d}^{q+1}f}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}^{q+1}f$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}^{q+1}f$}}\left(x-z\frac{t}{b}\right)t^{q+1}\psi\left(t\right)\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}z}{\hbox{\rm d}z}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}z$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}z$}}\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}t}{\hbox{\rm d}t}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}t$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}t$}}.

For all
z\in(0,1)
and
t\in H=\left[-\frac{r}{\sqrt{n}},\frac{r}{\sqrt{n}}\right]^{n}
,
we have
\left|\frac{zt}{b}\right|\leq\left|\frac{t}{b}\right|\leq\frac{\sqrt{n}|t|_{\infty}}{b}\leq\frac{rb}{b}=r

and hence
x-z\frac{t}{b}\in\overline{B}_{r}(x)
. Thereby, assumption
(
3.9
) yields
\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}^{q+1}f}{\hbox{\rm d}^{q+1}f}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}^{q+1}f$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}^{q+1}f$}}\left(x-z\frac{t}{b}\right)\leq Mc^{q+1}
,
and hence

\displaystyle\left|R_{q}\left(x\right)\right|

\displaystyle\leq b^{-q-1}\frac{Mc^{q+1}}{(q+1)!}\intop_{H}\left|t^{q+1}\psi\left(t\right)\right|\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}t}{\hbox{\rm d}t}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}t$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}t$}}=

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{b}{c}\right)^{-q-1}\frac{M}{(q+1)!}\intop_{[-1,1]^{n}}\left|t^{q+1}\psi\left(t\right)\right|\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}t}{\hbox{\rm d}t}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}t$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}t$}}\leq

\displaystyle\leq\left(\frac{b}{c}\right)^{-q-1}\frac{M}{(q+1)!}\intop_{[-1,1]^{n}}\left|\psi\left(t\right)\right|\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}t}{\hbox{\rm d}t}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}t$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}t$}}\leq

\displaystyle\leq\left(\frac{b}{c}\right)^{-q-1}\frac{2M}{(q+1)!},

where we used
(i)
and
(vi)

of Lem.
21
and
\frac{br}{\sqrt{n}}>1
. We
can now let
q\rightarrow+\infty
considering that
\frac{b}{c}>\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}^{-s}

for some
s\in\mathbb{R}_{>0}
, so that
\left|R_{q}\left(x\right)\right|\rightarrow 0

and hence
\left(\delta*f\right)(x)=f(x)
.
∎

Example 48
.

(i)

If
f_{\omega}(x)=e^{-ix\omega}
,
b\geq\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}^{-r}
and
\omega\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}

satisfies
|\omega|\leq\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}^{-s}
with
s<r
(e.g. if
\omega

is a weak infinite number, see Def.
3
), then
\frac{b}{|\omega|}\geq\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}^{-(r-s)}

and
f_{\omega}
is bounded by a tame polynomial at each point
x\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}
.
On the contrary, e.g. if
b=\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}^{-r}
and
\left|\omega\right|\geq\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}^{-r}
,
then
\frac{b}{|\omega|}\leq 1
and
f_{\omega}
is not bounded by
a tame polynomial at any
x\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}
.

(ii)

If
f\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GC}^{\infty}(\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{n})
has always finite derivatives at a finite
point
x\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
(e.g. it originates from the embedding of an
ordinary smooth function), then it suffices to take
c=\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}^{-r-1}

to prove that
f
is bounded by a tame polynomial at
x
. Similarly,
we can argue if
f
is polynomially bounded for
x\to\infty
and

x\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
is not finite.

(iii)

The Dirac delta
\delta(x)=b^{n}\psi(bx)
is not bounded by a tame
polynomial at
x=0
. This also shows that, generally speaking, the
embedding of a compactly supported distribution is not bounded by
a tame polynomial. Below we will show that indeed
\delta*\delta\neq\delta
,
even if we clearly have
\left(\delta*\delta\right)(x)=\delta(x)=0

for all
x\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
such that
|x|\geq r\in\mathbb{R}_{>0}
.

(iv)

If
f\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GC}^{\infty}\left(\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{n}\right)

is bounded by a tame polynomial at
0
, then since
\delta
is an
even function (see Example
24
.
(i)
),
we have:

\int\delta(x)\cdot f(x)\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}x}{\hbox{\rm d}x}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}x$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}x$}}=\int\delta(0-x)\cdot f(x)\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}x}{\hbox{\rm d}x}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}x$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}x$}}=\left(f*\delta\right)(0)=f(0).

(3.11)

Finally, the following theorem considers the relations between convolution
of distributions and their embedding as GSF:

Theorem 49
.

Let
S\in\mathcal{E}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
,
T\in\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{n})

and
b\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}
be a strong positive infinite number, then for
all
\varphi\in\mathcal{D}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
:

(i)

\langle S*T,\varphi\rangle=\int\iota_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}^{b}(S)(x)\cdot\iota_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}^{b}(T)(y)\cdot\varphi(x+y)\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}x}{\hbox{\rm d}x}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}x$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}x$}}\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}y}{\hbox{\rm d}y}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}y$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}y$}}=\int\left(\iota_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}^{b}(S)*\iota_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}^{b}(T)\right)(z)\cdot\varphi(z)\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}z}{\hbox{\rm d}z}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}z$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}z$}}.

(ii)

T*\varphi=\iota_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}^{b}(T)*\varphi
.

Proof.

(i)
: Using (
3.4
), we have

\displaystyle\langle S*T,\varphi\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle S(x),\langle T(y),\varphi(x+y)\rangle\rangle=\langle S(x),\int\iota_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}^{b}(T)(y)\varphi(x+y)\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}y}{\hbox{\rm d}y}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}y$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}y$}}\rangle=

\displaystyle=\int\iota_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}^{b}(S)(x)\int\iota_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}^{b}(T)(y)\varphi(x+y)\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}y}{\hbox{\rm d}y}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}y$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}y$}}\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}x}{\hbox{\rm d}x}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}x$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}x$}}=

\displaystyle=\int\left(\iota_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}^{b}(S)*\iota_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}^{b}(T)\right)(z)\varphi(z)\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}z}{\hbox{\rm d}z}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}z$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}z$}},

where, in the last step, we used the change of variables
x=z-y

and Fubini’s theorem.

(ii)
: For all
x\in{\text{\rm c}}({\mathbb{R}^{n}})
, using again (
3.4
),
we have
\left(T*\varphi\right)(x)=\langle T(y),\varphi(x-y)\rangle=\int\iota_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}^{b}(T)(y)\varphi(x-y)\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}y}{\hbox{\rm d}y}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}y$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}y$}}=\left(\iota_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}^{b}(T)*\varphi\right)(x)
.
∎

We note that an equality of the type
\iota_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}^{b}(S*T)=\iota_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}^{b}(S)*\iota_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}^{b}(T)

cannot hold because from Thm.
43
.
(ii)

it would imply
1*(\delta^{\prime}*H)=(1*\delta^{\prime})*H
as distributions. Considering
their embeddings, we have
\iota_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}^{b}(1)*\left(\iota_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}^{b}(\delta^{\prime})*\iota_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}^{b}(H)\right)=\iota_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}^{b}(1)*\left(\iota_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}^{b}(\delta)*\iota_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}^{b}(\delta)\right)=\left(\iota_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}^{b}(1)*\iota_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}^{b}(\delta^{\prime})\right)*\iota_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}^{b}(H)=\left(\iota_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}^{b}(1^{\prime})*\iota_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}^{b}(\delta)\right)*\iota_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}^{b}(H)=0
.
In particular, at the term
\iota_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}^{b}(\delta)*\iota_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}^{b}(\delta)

we cannot apply Thm.
47
because
\delta^{(j)}(x)=b^{j+1}\psi^{(j)}(bx)
.
This also implies that
\iota_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}^{b}(\delta)*\iota_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}^{b}(\delta)\neq\iota_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}^{b}(\delta)

because otherwise we would have
0=\iota_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}^{b}(1)*\left(\iota_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}^{b}(\delta)*\iota_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}^{b}(\delta)\right)=\iota_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}^{b}(1)*\iota_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}^{b}(\delta)=\int\delta=1
.

4.
Hyperfinite Fourier transform

Definition 50
.

Let
k\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}
be a positive infinite
number. Let
f\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GC}^{\infty}(K,\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{C}})
, we define the
n
-dimensional

hyperfinite Fourier transform (HFT)
\mathcal{F}_{k}(f)
of

f

on
K:=\left[-k,k\right]^{n}
as follows:

\mathcal{F}_{k}\left(f\right)\left(\omega\right):=\intop_{K}f\left(x\right)e^{-ix\cdotp\omega}\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}x}{\hbox{\rm d}x}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}x$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}x$}}=\intop_{-k}^{k}\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}x_{1}}{\hbox{\rm d}x_{1}}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}x_{1}$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}x_{1}$}}\ldots\intop_{-k}^{k}f\left(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n}\right)e^{-ix\cdotp\omega}\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}x_{n}}{\hbox{\rm d}x_{n}}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}x_{n}$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}x_{n}$}},

(4.1)

where
x=\left(x_{1}\ldots x_{n}\right)\in K
and
\omega=\left(\omega_{1}\ldots\omega_{n}\right)\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
.
As usual, the product
x\cdotp\omega
on
\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
denotes the
dot product
x\cdotp\omega=\sum_{j=1}^{n}x_{j}\omega_{j}\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}
.
For simplicity, in the following we will also use the notation
\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GC}^{\infty}(X):=\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GC}^{\infty}(X,\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{C}})
.
If
f\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{D}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{D}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{D}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{D}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GD}(X)
and
\text{supp}(f)\subseteq K=[-k,k]^{n}
, based
on Def.
40
, we can use the simplified notation

\mathcal{F}(f):=\mathcal{F}_{k}(f)
.

In the following,
k=[k_{\varepsilon}]\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}
will always denote
a positive infinite number, and we set
K:=\left[-k,k\right]^{n}\Subset_{\text{\rm f}}\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
.

The adjective
hyperfinite
can be motivated as follows: on the
one hand,
k\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}
is an infinite number, but on the other hand
we already mentioned that GSF behave on a functionally compact set
like
K
as if it were a compact set. Similarly to the case of hyperfinite
numbers
\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}
(see Def.
34
), the adjective

hyperfinite
is frequently used to denote mathematical objects
which are in some sense infinite but behave, from several points of
view, as bounded ones.

Theorem 51
.

Let
f\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GC}^{\infty}\left(K\right)
, then the following
properties hold:

(i)

Let
\omega=[\omega_{\varepsilon}]\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
and
let
f
be defined by the net
(f_{\varepsilon})
. Then we have:

\mathcal{F}_{k}\left(f\right)\left(\omega\right)=\left[\intop_{-k_{\varepsilon}}^{k_{\varepsilon}}\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}x_{1}}{\hbox{\rm d}x_{1}}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}x_{1}$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}x_{1}$}}\ldots\intop_{-k_{\varepsilon}}^{k_{\varepsilon}}f_{\varepsilon}\left(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n}\right)e^{-ix\cdotp\omega_{\varepsilon}}\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}x_{n}}{\hbox{\rm d}x_{n}}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}x_{n}$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}x_{n}$}}\right]=\left[\hat{\mathcal{F}}(\chi_{K_{\varepsilon}}f_{\varepsilon})(\omega_{\varepsilon})\right]\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{C}},

where
\hat{\mathcal{F}}:\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{n})\longrightarrow\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{n})

is the classical FT, and
\chi_{K_{\varepsilon}}
is the characteristic
function of
K_{\varepsilon}
.

(ii)

\forall\omega\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{n}:\ \left|\mathcal{F}_{k}(f)(\omega)\right|\leq\int_{K}\left|f(x)\right|\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}x}{\hbox{\rm d}x}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}x$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}x$}}=\|f\|_{1}
,
so that the HFT is always sharply bounded.

(iii)

\mathcal{F}_{k}:\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GC}^{\infty}\left(K\right)\longrightarrow\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GC}^{\infty}\left(\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{n}\right)
.

Proof.

(i)
: For all
\omega\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
fixed, the map

x\in K\mapsto f\left(x\right)e^{-ix\cdotp\omega}
is a GSF by the
closure with respect to composition, i.e. Thm.
13
.
(iv)
.
Therefore, we can apply Thm.
33
.
(v)
.

To prove
(iii)
, we have to show that
\mathcal{F}_{k}(f):\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{n}\longrightarrow\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{C}}

is defined by a net
\left(\mathcal{F}_{k}\right)_{\varepsilon}\in\mathcal{C}^{\infty}\left(\mathbb{R}^{n},\mathbb{C}\right)

(see Def.
12
). We can naturally define such a net
as

\left(\mathcal{F}_{k}\right)_{\varepsilon}\left(y\right):=\intop_{-k_{\varepsilon}}^{k_{\varepsilon}}\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}x_{1}}{\hbox{\rm d}x_{1}}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}x_{1}$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}x_{1}$}}\ldots\intop_{-k_{\varepsilon}}^{k_{\varepsilon}}f_{\varepsilon}\left(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n}\right)e^{-ix\cdotp y}\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}x_{n}}{\hbox{\rm d}x_{n}}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}x_{n}$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}x_{n}$}}\quad\forall y\in\mathbb{R}^{n},

and we claim it satisfies the following properties:

(a)

\left[\left(\mathcal{F}_{k}\right)_{\varepsilon}\left(\omega_{\varepsilon}\right)\right]\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{C}}
,

\forall\left[\omega_{\varepsilon}\right]\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
.

(b)

\forall\left[\omega_{\varepsilon}\right]\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{n}\,\forall\alpha\in\mathbb{N}^{n}:\ \left(\partial^{\alpha}\left(\mathcal{F}_{k}\right)_{\varepsilon}\left(\omega_{\varepsilon}\right)\right)\in\mathbb{C}_{\rho}
.

Claim
(a)
is justified by
(i)
above.
From
(i)
it directly follows
(ii)
. In
order to prove
(b)
, we use the standard derivation
under the integral sign to have

\partial^{\alpha}\left(\mathcal{F}_{k}\right)_{\varepsilon}\left(\omega_{\varepsilon}\right)=\intop_{-k_{\varepsilon}}^{k_{\varepsilon}}\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}x_{1}}{\hbox{\rm d}x_{1}}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}x_{1}$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}x_{1}$}}\ldots\intop_{-k_{\varepsilon}}^{k_{\varepsilon}}f_{\varepsilon}\left(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n}\right)e^{-ix\cdotp\omega_{\varepsilon}}(-ix^{\alpha})\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}x_{n}}{\hbox{\rm d}x_{n}}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}x_{n}$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}x_{n}$}}.

We can now proceed as above to prove
(b)
and hence
the claim
(iii)
.
∎

4.1.
The heuristic motivation
of the FT in a non-Archimedean setting

Frequently, the formula for the definition of the FT (e.g. for rapidly
decreasing functions) is informally motivated using its relations
with Fourier series. In order to replicate a similar argument for
GSF, we need the notion of
hyperseries
. In fact, exactly as
the ordinary limit
\lim_{n\in\mathbb{N}}a_{n}
is not well suited for the
sharp topology (because of its infinitesimal neighbourhoods) and we
have to consider hyperlimits
\mathchoice{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\raisebox{-0.39864pt}{$\rho\hskip-5.49028pt$}$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\raisebox{-0.39864pt}{$\rho\hskip-5.49028pt$}$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\raisebox{-0.39864pt}{$\rho\hskip-5.49028pt$}$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\raisebox{-0.39864pt}{$\rho\hskip-5.49028pt$}$}}}\lim_{n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}\,}}{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}\lim_{n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}\,}}{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}\lim_{n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}\,}}{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}\lim_{n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}\,}}a_{n}
(see
Def.
34
.
(iii)
), likewise to
study series of
a_{n}\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{C}}
,
n\in\mathbb{N}
, we have to consider

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{$\rho\hskip-5.49028pt$}$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{$\rho\hskip-5.49028pt$}$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{$\rho\hskip-5.49028pt$}$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{$\rho\hskip-5.49028pt$}$}}}\sum_{n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}\,}}{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}\sum_{n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}\,}}{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}\sum_{n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}\,}}{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}\sum_{n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}\,}}a_{n}

\displaystyle:=\mathchoice{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\raisebox{-0.39864pt}{$\rho\hskip-5.49028pt$}$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\raisebox{-0.39864pt}{$\rho\hskip-5.49028pt$}$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\raisebox{-0.39864pt}{$\rho\hskip-5.49028pt$}$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\raisebox{-0.39864pt}{$\rho\hskip-5.49028pt$}$}}}\lim_{N\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}\,}}{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}\lim_{N\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}\,}}{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}\lim_{N\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}\,}}{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}\lim_{N\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}\,}}\sum_{n=0}^{N}a_{n}\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{C}},

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{$\rho\hskip-5.49028pt$}$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{$\rho\hskip-5.49028pt$}$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{$\rho\hskip-5.49028pt$}$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{$\rho\hskip-5.49028pt$}$}}}\sum_{n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{Z}}\,}}{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}\sum_{n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{Z}}\,}}{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}\sum_{n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{Z}}\,}}{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}\sum_{n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{Z}}\,}}a_{n}

\displaystyle:=\mathchoice{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\raisebox{-0.39864pt}{$\rho\hskip-5.49028pt$}$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\raisebox{-0.39864pt}{$\rho\hskip-5.49028pt$}$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\raisebox{-0.39864pt}{$\rho\hskip-5.49028pt$}$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\raisebox{-0.39864pt}{$\rho\hskip-5.49028pt$}$}}}\lim_{N\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}\,}}{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}\lim_{N\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}\,}}{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}\lim_{N\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}\,}}{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}\lim_{N\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}\,}}\sum_{n=-N}^{N}a_{n}\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{C}},

where
\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{Z}}:=\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}\cup\left(-\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}\right)\subseteq\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}
.
The main problem in this definition is how to define the
hyperfinite
sums

\sum_{n=M}^{N}a_{n}\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{C}}
for arbitrary hypernatural
numbers
N
,
M\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}
and starting from suitable
ordinary

sequences
\left(a_{n}\right)_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
of
\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{C}}
. However, this
can be done, and the resulting notion extends several classical theorems,
see
[
62
]
.

Only for this section, we hence assume that
f\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{D}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{D}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{D}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{D}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GD}([-T,T])
,

T\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}
, can be written as a Fourier hyperseries

f(t)=\mathchoice{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{$\rho\hskip-5.49028pt$}$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{$\rho\hskip-5.49028pt$}$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{$\rho\hskip-5.49028pt$}$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{$\rho\hskip-5.49028pt$}$}}}\sum_{n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{Z}}\,}}{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}\sum_{n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{Z}}\,}}{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}\sum_{n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{Z}}\,}}{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}\sum_{n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{Z}}\,}}c_{n}e^{2\pi i\frac{n}{T}t}\quad\forall t\in(-T,T),

where
\sigma
is another gauge such that
\sigma_{\varepsilon}\leq\rho_{\varepsilon}^{q}

for all
q\in\mathbb{N}
and for
\varepsilon
small (so that
\mathbb{R}_{\rho}\subseteq\mathbb{R}_{\sigma}
,
see Def.
1
). Using Thm.
35
to exchange
hyperseries and integration, for each
h\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{Z}}
, we have

\int_{-T}^{T}f(t)e^{-2\pi i\frac{h}{T}t}\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}t}{\hbox{\rm d}t}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}t$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}t$}}=\mathchoice{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{$\rho\hskip-5.49028pt$}$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{$\rho\hskip-5.49028pt$}$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{$\rho\hskip-5.49028pt$}$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{$\rho\hskip-5.49028pt$}$}}}\sum_{n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{Z}}\,}}{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}\sum_{n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{Z}}\,}}{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}\sum_{n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{Z}}\,}}{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}\sum_{n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{Z}}\,}}c_{n}\int_{-T}^{T}e^{2\pi i\frac{t}{T}(n-h)}\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}t}{\hbox{\rm d}t}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}t$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}t$}}=2T\cdot c_{h}.

That is
c_{h}=\frac{1}{2T}\mathcal{F}(f)\left(2\pi\frac{h}{T}\right)
.

It is also well-known that, informally, if
T
is “sufficiently
large”, then the Fourier coefficients
c_{n}
“approximate”
the FT scaled by
\frac{1}{2T}
and dilated by
2\pi
. Using our
non-Archimedean language, this can be formalized as follows: Let
\omega=[\omega_{\varepsilon}]\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}
,
and assume that
T=[T_{\varepsilon}]
is an infinite number, then setting

h_{\omega}:=\left[\text{int}\left(\omega_{\varepsilon}\cdot T_{\varepsilon}\right)\right]\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{Z}}

(here we use
\mathbb{R}_{\rho}\subseteq\mathbb{R}_{\sigma}
), we have
\omega_{\varepsilon}\leq\frac{h_{\omega\varepsilon}}{T_{\varepsilon}}\leq\omega_{\varepsilon}+\frac{1}{T_{\varepsilon}}
,
so that
\frac{h_{\omega}}{T}\approx\omega
because
T
is an infinite
number. By Thm.
51
,
\mathcal{F}(f)
is a GSF. Let

a
,
b
,
c
,
d\in
\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}
, with
a<c<d<b
, and set
M:=\max_{\omega\in[2\pi a,2\pi b]}\mathcal{F}(f)^{\prime}(\omega)
.
Using Lem.
4
, we can find
q\in\mathbb{N}
such that
c-a\geq\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}^{q}

and
b-d\geq\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}^{q}
. Assume that
T
is sufficiently large
so that the following conditions hold

\frac{1}{T}\leq\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}^{q},\quad\frac{M}{T}\approx 0.

Then, for all
\omega\in[c,d]
, we have
\frac{h_{\omega}}{T}\leq\omega+\frac{1}{T}\leq d+\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}^{q}\leq b
,
and
\frac{h_{\omega}}{T}\geq\omega\geq c>a
, so that
\frac{h_{\omega}}{T}
,

\omega\in[a,b]
. From the mean value theorem Thm.
31
,
we hence have

\left|\mathcal{F}(f)\left(2\pi\frac{h_{\omega}}{T}\right)-\mathcal{F}(f)\left(2\pi\omega\right)\right|\leq 2\pi M\left|\frac{h_{\omega}}{T}-\omega\right|\leq 2\pi\frac{M}{T}\approx 0.

We hence proved that

\exists Q\in\mathbb{N}\,\forall T\geq\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}^{-Q}:\ c_{h_{\omega}}\approx\frac{1}{2T}\mathcal{F}(f)(2\pi\omega).

Finally, note that since
T
is an infinite number, if
h_{\omega}\in\mathbb{Z}
,
then necessarily
\omega
must be infinitesimal; on the contrary,
if
\omega\geq r\in\mathbb{R}_{\neq 0}
, then necessarily
h_{\omega}\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{Z}}\setminus\mathbb{Z}

is an infinite integer number.

Therefore, with the precise meaning given above, the heuristic relations
between Fourier coefficients and HFT holds also for GSF.

5.
The Riemann-Lebesgue lemma
in a non-linear setting

The following result represents the Riemann-Lebesgue lemma in our
framework. It immediately highlights an important difference with
respect to the classical approach since it states that the HFT of
a very large class of compactly supported GSF is still compactly supported
(see also Thm.
69
for a classical formulation
of the uncertainty inequality for GSF).

Lemma 52
.

Let
H\Subset_{\text{\rm f}}\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
and
f\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{D}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{D}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{D}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{D}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GD}\left(H\right)

be a compactly supported GSF. Assume that

\exists C,b\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\,\forall x\in H\,\forall j\in\mathbb{N}:\ \left|\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}^{j}}{\hbox{\rm d}^{j}}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}^{j}$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}^{j}$}}f(x)\right|\leq C\cdot b^{j}.

(5.1)

For all
N_{1},\ldots,N_{n}\in\mathbb{N}
and
\omega\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
, if
\omega_{1}^{N_{1}}\cdot\ldots\cdot\omega_{n}^{N_{n}}

is invertible, then

\left|\mathcal{F}(f)(\omega)\right|\leq\frac{1}{\left|\omega_{1}^{N_{1}}\cdot\ldots\cdot\omega_{n}^{N_{n}}\right|}\cdot\int_{H}\left|\partial_{1}^{N_{1}}\ldots\partial_{n}^{N_{n}}f(x)\right|\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}x}{\hbox{\rm d}x}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}x$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}x$}}.

(5.2)

Therefore

\lim_{\omega\to\infty}\left|\mathcal{F}(f)(\omega)\right|=0.

(5.3)

Actually, (
5.2
) yields the stronger result:

\exists Q\in\mathbb{N}:\ \mathcal{F}(f)\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{D}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{D}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{D}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{D}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GD}\left(\overline{B_{\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}^{-Q}}(0)}\right).

(5.4)

Proof.

Let us apply integration by parts Thm.
18
.
(vi)

at the
p
-th integral in (
4.1
) (assuming that

N_{p}>0
):

\displaystyle\intop_{-k}^{k}f\left(x\right)e^{-i\omega\cdot x}\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}x_{p}}{\hbox{\rm d}x_{p}}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}x_{p}$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}x_{p}$}}

\displaystyle=\left.-\frac{f\left(x\right)}{i\omega_{p}}e^{-i\omega\cdot x}\right|_{x_{p}=-k}^{x_{p}=k}+\frac{1}{i\omega_{p}}\intop_{-k}^{k}\partial_{p}f\left(x\right)e^{-i\omega\cdot x}\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}x_{p}}{\hbox{\rm d}x_{p}}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}x_{p}$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}x_{p}$}}=

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{i\omega_{p}}\intop_{-k}^{k}\partial_{p}f\left(x\right)e^{-i\omega\cdot x}\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}x_{p}}{\hbox{\rm d}x_{p}}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}x_{p}$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}x_{p}$}}.

because Thm.
39
.
(iii)

yields
f(x)=0
if
x_{p}=\pm k
. Applying the same idea with
N_{p}\in\mathbb{N}

repeated integrations by parts for each integral in (
4.1
),
and using Thm.
39
.
(iii)
,
we obtain

\mathcal{F}(f)(\omega)=\frac{1}{\omega_{1}^{N_{1}}\cdot\ldots\cdot\omega_{n}^{N_{n}}i^{N_{1}+\ldots+N_{n}}}\int_{K}\partial_{1}^{N_{1}}\ldots\partial_{n}^{N_{n}}f(x)e^{-ix\cdot\omega}\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}.

Claims (
5.2
) and (
5.3
) both follows from
Thm.
33
.
(iv)

and from the closure of GSF with respect to differentiation, i.e. Thm.
14
.

To prove (
5.4
), we first recall (
2.3
),
so that
\overline{B_{\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}^{-Q}}(0)}\Subset_{\text{\rm f}}\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
. Let
C
,

b\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}
from (
5.1
) and
\lambda(H)\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}
,
where
\lambda
is the Lebesgue measure. Therefore,
b\leq\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}^{-R}

for some
R\in\mathbb{N}
, and we can set
Q:=R+1
. We want to prove the
claim using Thm.
39
.
(i)
,
so that we take
\omega=(\omega_{1},\ldots,\omega_{n})\in\text{ext}\left(\overline{B_{\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}^{-Q}}(0)}\right)
.
It cannot be
|\omega|<_{\text{\rm s}}\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}^{-Q}
because this would
yield
|\omega-a|=_{\text{\rm s}}0
for some
a\in\overline{B_{\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}^{-Q}}(0)}
;
thereby,
|\omega|\geq\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}^{-Q}
by Lem.
9
.
It always holds
\max_{l=1,\ldots,n}|\omega_{l}|\geq\frac{1}{n}|\omega|
,
i.e.
\left[\max_{l=1,\ldots,n}|\omega_{l\varepsilon}|\right]\geq\frac{1}{n}\left[|\omega_{\varepsilon}|\right]
,
where
\omega_{l}=[\omega_{l\varepsilon}]
and
\omega_{\varepsilon}:=\left|(\omega_{1\varepsilon},\ldots,\omega_{n\varepsilon})\right|
.
In general, we cannot say that
|\omega_{p}|=\max_{l=1,\ldots,n}|\omega_{l}|

for some
p=1,\ldots,n
because at most this equality holds only
for subpoints. In fact, set
L_{p}:=\left\{\varepsilon\in I\mid\max_{l=1,\ldots,n}|\omega_{l\varepsilon}|=\left|\omega_{p\varepsilon}\right|\right\}

and let
P\subseteq\{1,\ldots,n\}
be the non empty set of all the
indices
p=1,\ldots,n
such that
L_{p}\subseteq_{0}I
. We hence have

|\omega_{p}|=_{L_{p}}\max_{l=1,\ldots,n}|\omega_{l}|\geq\frac{1}{n}|\omega|\geq\frac{1}{n}\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}^{-Q}

for all
p\in P
, and

\forall^{0}\varepsilon\,\exists p\in P:\ \varepsilon\in L_{p}.

(5.5)

We apply assumption (
5.1
) and inequality (
5.2
)
with an arbitrary
N_{p}=N\in\mathbb{N}
,
p\in P
, and with
N_{j}=0

for all
j\neq p
to get

\displaystyle\left|\mathcal{F}(f)(\omega)\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{|\omega_{p}|^{N}}\cdot\int_{H}\left|\partial_{p}^{N}f(x)\right|\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}x}{\hbox{\rm d}x}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}x$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}x$}}\leq_{L_{p}}n^{N}\cdot\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}^{NQ}Cb^{N}\lambda(H)\leq

\displaystyle\leq\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}^{-1}\cdot\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}^{N(Q-R)}C\lambda(H)=\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}^{N-1}C\lambda(H).

For
N\to+\infty
(in the ring
\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}|_{L_{p}})
, we hence have that

\mathcal{F}(f)(\omega)=_{L_{p}}0
. From (
5.5
) we hence
finally get
\mathcal{F}(f)(\omega)=0
.
∎

Remark 53
.

(i)

Considering that
\delta(t)=b^{n}\psi(bt)
and that
\psi
is an
even function (Lem.
21
.
(i)
),
we have

\mathcal{F}(\delta)(\omega)=\int\delta(t)e^{-it\omega}\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}t}{\hbox{\rm d}t}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}t$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}t$}}=\int\delta(0-t)e^{-it\omega}\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}t}{\hbox{\rm d}t}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}t$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}t$}}=\left(\delta*e^{-i(-)\omega}\right)(0).

(5.6)

We already know that if
b/|\omega|
is a strong infinite number,
then the function
f_{\omega}(t)=e^{-it\omega}
is bounded by a tame
polynomial. Thereby, using Thm.
47
, we have

\mathcal{F}(\delta)(\omega)=f_{\omega}(0)=1
; in particular,
\mathcal{F}(\delta)|_{\mathbb{R}}=1
.

(ii)

On the other hand (taking for simplicity
\psi:=\mathcal{F}^{-1}(\beta)
,
where
\beta\in\mbox{$\mathcal{C}$}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})
is supported e.g. in
[-1,1]
and identically
equals
1
in a neighborhood of
0
, see Thm.
22
),

\delta^{(j)}(t)=b^{j+1}\psi^{(j)}(bt)
if
n=1
, and hence for
all
t\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}
, we have

\displaystyle\delta^{(j)}(t)

\displaystyle=b^{j+1}\psi^{(j)}(bt)=b^{j+1}\cdot\left[\frac{\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}}{\hbox{\rm d}}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}$}}^{j}}{\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}t^{j}}{\hbox{\rm d}t^{j}}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}t^{j}$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}t^{j}$}}}\left(\frac{1}{2\pi}\int\beta(x)e^{ib_{\varepsilon}tx}\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}x}{\hbox{\rm d}x}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}x$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}x$}}\right)\right]=

\displaystyle=\frac{b^{j+1}}{2\pi}\left[\int(ib_{\varepsilon}x)^{j}\beta(x)e^{ib_{\varepsilon}tx}\ \mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}x}{\hbox{\rm d}x}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}x$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}x$}}\right]

\displaystyle\left|\delta^{(j)}(t)\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{b^{2j+1}}{2\pi}\int_{-1}^{1}|x|^{j}\beta(x)\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}x}{\hbox{\rm d}x}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}x$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}x$}}=:C(b^{2})^{j}.

Thus, Dirac’s delta satisfies condition (
5.1
) and hence

\exists Q\in\mathbb{N}:\ \mathcal{F}(\delta)\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{D}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{D}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{D}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{D}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GD}(\overline{B_{\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}^{-Q}}(0)}).

(5.7)

In the following, we will use the notation
\mathbb{1}:=\mathcal{F}(\delta)
.

(iii)

The previous result also yields that
f*\delta=f
cannot hold in
