general since otherwise, we can argue as in (
5.6
)
to prove that
\mathcal{F}(\delta)(\omega)=1
for all
\omega\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}
,
in contradiction with (
5.7
).

Inequality (
5.2
) can also be stated as a general impossibility
theorem (where we intuitively think
n=1
).

Theorem 54
.

Let
(R,\leq)
be an ordered ring and
G
be an

R
-module. Assume that we have the following maps (for which we
use notations aiming to draw the interpretation where
G
is a space
of GF)

\displaystyle(-)^{\prime}

\displaystyle:G\longrightarrow G

\displaystyle\int

\displaystyle:G\longrightarrow R

\displaystyle(-)\cdot\exp_{\omega}

\displaystyle:G\longrightarrow G\quad\forall\omega\in R

\displaystyle|-|

\displaystyle:R\longrightarrow R.

These maps satisfy the following integration by parts formula

\int f\cdot\exp_{\omega}=\frac{1}{\omega}\int f^{\prime}\cdot\exp_{\omega}

(5.8)

for all invertible
\omega\in R^{*}
,
f\in G
, and

|rs|=|r||s|\quad\forall r,s\in R

(5.9)

\forall f\in G\,\exists C\in R\,\forall\omega\in R^{*}:\ \left|\int f\cdot\exp_{\omega}\right|\leq C.

(5.10)

Then for all
f\in G
and all
N\in\mathbb{N}_{>0}
there exists
C=C(f,N)\in R

such that

\forall\omega\in R^{*}:\left|\int f\cdot\exp_{\omega}\right|\leq\frac{C}{|\omega|^{N}}.

(5.11)

Therefore, if
\delta\in G
satisfies
\frac{C(\delta,N)}{|\omega|^{N}}<1

for some
\omega\in R
and some
N\in\mathbb{N}
, then

\left|\int\delta\cdot\exp_{\omega}\right|<1.

Proof.

For
f\in G
, in the usual way we recursively define
f^{(p)}\in G

using the map
(-)^{\prime}:G\longrightarrow G
. Taking formula (
5.8
)
for
N\in\mathbb{N}_{>0}
times we get
\int f\cdot\exp_{\omega}=\frac{1}{\omega^{N}}\int f^{(N)}\cdot\exp_{\omega}
.
Applying
|-|
and using (
5.9
) and (
5.10
)
we get the conclusion (
5.11
).
∎

Note that we can take
R=\left\{i\cdot r\mid r\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{C}}\right\}

to apply this abstract result to the case of Lem.
52
.
This result also underscore that in the case
G=\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R})
,

R=\mathbb{R}
we cannot have an integration by parts formula such as (
5.8
).
Once more, it also underscores that, since (
5.8
)
holds in our setting, we cannot have
f*\delta=f
without limitations
because this would imply
\mathcal{F}(\delta)(\omega)=1
for all

\omega\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}
.

Example 55
.

Let
f\left(x\right)=e^{x}
for all
\left|x\right|\leq k
,
where
k:=-\log\left(\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}\right)
. The hyperfinite Fourier transform

\mathcal{F}_{k}
of
f
is

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{k}\left(f\right)\left(\omega\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{e^{k\left(1-i\omega\right)}-e^{-k\left(1-i\omega\right)}}{1-i\omega}=\frac{\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\rho^{\left(i\omega-1\right)}}{\hbox{\rm d}\rho^{\left(i\omega-1\right)}}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho^{\left(i\omega-1\right)}$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho^{\left(i\omega-1\right)}$}}-\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\rho^{\left(1-i\omega\right)}}{\hbox{\rm d}\rho^{\left(1-i\omega\right)}}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho^{\left(1-i\omega\right)}$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho^{\left(1-i\omega\right)}$}}}{1-i\omega}=

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{1-i\omega}\left(\frac{\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}^{i\omega}}{\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}}-\frac{\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}}{\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}^{i\omega}}\right)\quad\forall\omega\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}.

Note that
1-i\omega
,
\omega\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}
, is always invertible with
the usual inverse
\frac{1+i\omega}{1+\omega^{2}}
, moreover,
\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}^{i\omega}=e^{i\omega\log\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}}

and hence
|\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}^{i\omega}|=1
. Therefore,
\mathcal{F}_{k}(f)(\omega)

is always an infinite complex number for all finite numbers
\omega
.
If
\omega\geq\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}^{-1-r}
,
r\in\mathbb{R}_{>0}
, then
\mathcal{F}_{k}(f)(\omega)

is infinitesimal but not zero. Clearly,
f\notin\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{D}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{D}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{D}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{D}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GD}(K)
.

considering Robinson-Colombeau generalized numbers, the Gaussian
is compactly supported:

Lemma 56
.

Let
f\left(x\right)=e^{-\frac{\left|x\right|^{2}}{2}}

for all
x\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
. Then
f\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{D}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{D}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{D}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{D}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GD}\left(\overline{B_{h}(0)}\right)

for all strong infinite number
h\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}
. Moreover,
\mathcal{F}\left(f\right)=\left(2\pi\right)^{\frac{n}{2}}f
.

Proof.

The function
f
satisfies the inequality
0\leq f\left(x\right)\leq\left|x\right|^{-q}
,

\forall q\in\mathbb{N}
, for
\left|x\right|
finite sufficiently
large. Therefore, for all strongly infinite
x
, we have
f\left(x\right)=0

i.e.,
f\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{D}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{D}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{D}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{D}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GD}\left(\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{n}\right)
. We first prove the second
claim in dimension
n=1
; denoting by
\hat{\mathcal{F}}
the classical
Fourier we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}(f)(\omega)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{F}_{\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}^{-1}}(f)(\omega)=\int_{-\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}^{-1}}^{\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}^{-1}}e^{-x^{2}/2}e^{-i\omega x}\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}x}{\hbox{\rm d}x}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}x$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}x$}}=

\displaystyle=\left[\int_{-\rho_{\varepsilon}^{-1}}^{\rho_{\varepsilon}^{-1}}e^{-x^{2}/2}e^{-i\omega_{\varepsilon}x}\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}x}{\hbox{\rm d}x}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}x$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}x$}}\right]

\displaystyle=\left[\int_{-\rho_{\varepsilon}^{-1}}^{-\infty}e^{-x^{2}/2}e^{-i\omega_{\varepsilon}x}\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}x}{\hbox{\rm d}x}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}x$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}x$}}+\hat{\mathcal{F}}\left(e^{-x^{2}/2}\right)(\omega_{\varepsilon})+\int_{+\infty}^{\rho_{\varepsilon}^{-1}}e^{-x^{2}/2}e^{-i\omega_{\varepsilon}x}\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}x}{\hbox{\rm d}x}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}x$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}x$}}\right]

\displaystyle=\left[\sqrt{2\pi}e^{-\omega_{\varepsilon}^{2}/2}-2\int_{\rho_{\varepsilon}^{-1}}^{+\infty}e^{-x^{2}/2}e^{-i\omega_{\varepsilon}x}\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}x}{\hbox{\rm d}x}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}x$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}x$}}\right]

\displaystyle=\sqrt{2\pi}f(\omega)-2\cdot\left[\int_{\rho_{\varepsilon}^{-1}}^{+\infty}e^{-x^{2}/2}e^{-i\omega_{\varepsilon}x}\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}x}{\hbox{\rm d}x}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}x$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}x$}}\right].

Using L’Hôpital rule we can prove that
\lim_{y\to 0^{+}}\frac{\intop_{1/y}^{\pm\infty}e^{-\frac{x^{2}}{2}}\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}x}{\hbox{\rm d}x}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}x$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}x$}}}{y^{q}}=0

for all
q\in\mathbb{N}
, thereby
\left[\int_{\rho_{\varepsilon}^{-1}}^{+\infty}e^{-x^{2}/2}e^{-i\omega_{\varepsilon}x}\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}x}{\hbox{\rm d}x}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}x$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}x$}}\right]=0

in
\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}
. In dimension
n>1
, we directly calculate using Fubini’s
theorem:

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}\left(e^{-\frac{\left|x\right|^{2}}{2}}\right)\left(\omega\right)

\displaystyle=\prod_{j=1}^{n}\intop e^{-ix_{j}\cdot\omega_{j}}e^{-\frac{x_{j}^{2}}{2}}\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}x_{j}}{\hbox{\rm d}x_{j}}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}x_{j}$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}x_{j}$}}

\displaystyle=\prod_{j=1}^{n}\mathcal{F}\left(e^{-\frac{x_{j}^{2}}{2}}\right)\left(\omega_{j}\right)=\prod_{j=1}^{n}\left(2\pi\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}e^{-\frac{\omega_{j}^{2}}{2}}=\left(2\pi\right)^{\frac{n}{2}}e^{-\frac{\left|\omega\right|^{2}}{2}}.

∎

6.
Elementary properties of the hyperfinite Fourier transform

In this section, we list and prove elementary properties of the HFT.

Theorem 57
.

(see Sec.
2.6
for the
notations
\odot
and
\oplus
) Let
f\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GC}^{\infty}\left(K\right)
and

g:\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{n}\longrightarrow\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{C}}
, then

(i)

\mathcal{F}_{k}\left(f+g\right)=\mathcal{F}_{k}\left(f\right)+\mathcal{F}_{k}\left(g\right)

if
g\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GC}^{\infty}(K)
.

(ii)

\mathcal{F}_{k}\left(bf\right)=b\mathcal{F}_{k}\left(f\right)

for all
b\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{C}}
.

(iii)

\mathcal{F}_{k}\left(\overline{f}\right)=\overline{-1\diamond\mathcal{F}_{k}(f)}
,
where
-1\diamond f
is the
reflection
of
f
, i.e.
\left(-1\diamond f\right)\left(x\right):=f\left(-x\right)
.

(iv)

\mathcal{F}_{k}\left(-1\diamond f\right)=-1\diamond\mathcal{F}_{k}(f)

(v)

\mathcal{F}_{k}\left(t\diamond g\right)=t\odot\mathcal{F}_{tk}\left(g\right)

for all
t\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}
such that
tk
is still infinite and
g|_{K}\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GC}^{\infty}(K)
,

g|_{tK}\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GC}^{\infty}(tK)
. Here,
t\diamond g
is the
dilation

of
f,
i.e.
\left(t\diamond g\right)\left(x\right):=g\left(tx\right)
.

(vi)

Let
k>h>0
be infinite numbers,
s\in[-(k-h),k-h]^{n}
,

f\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{D}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{D}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{D}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{D}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GD}([-h,h]^{n})
. Then

\mathcal{F}_{k}\left(s\oplus f\right)=e^{-is\cdot\left(-\right)}\mathcal{F}_{k}\left(f\right)=e^{-is\cdot\left(-\right)}\mathcal{F}_{h}\left(f\right)=e^{-is\cdot\left(-\right)}\mathcal{F}\left(f\right).

In particular, if
h\geq\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}^{-p}
,
k\geq\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}^{-q}
,

p
,
q\in\mathbb{R}_{>0}
,
q>p
, and
s\in{\text{\rm c}}({\mathbb{R}^{n}})
, then
s\in[-(k-h),k-h]^{n}
.
In particular,
\mathbb{R}^{n}\subseteq[-(k-h),k-h]^{n}
.

(vii)

\mathcal{F}_{k}\left(e^{is\cdot\left(-\right)}f\right)=s\oplus\mathcal{F}_{k}\left(f\right)

for all
s\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
.

(viii)

Let
\omega\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
and
\alpha\in\mathbb{N}^{n}\setminus\{0\}
.
For
p=1,\ldots,|\alpha|
, define
\beta_{p}=\left(\beta_{p,q}\right)_{q=1,\ldots,n}\in\mathbb{N}^{n}

with

\displaystyle\beta_{0}

\displaystyle:=\alpha

\displaystyle\beta_{p+1}

\displaystyle:=(0,\displaystyle\mathop{\ldots\ldots\,}^{j_{p}-1},0,\beta_{p,j_{p}}-1,\beta_{p,j_{p}+1},\ldots,\beta_{p,n})\text{ if }j_{p}:=\min\left\{q\mid\beta_{p,q}>0\right\}.

Finally, for all
\bar{f}\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GC}^{\infty}(K)
and
j=1,\ldots,n
, set

\displaystyle\Delta_{1k}\bar{f}(\omega):=

\displaystyle\left[\bar{f}(x)e^{-ix\cdot\omega}\right]_{x_{1}=-k}^{x_{1}=k}

\displaystyle\Delta_{jk}\bar{f}(\omega):=

\displaystyle\intop_{-k}^{k}\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}x_{1}}{\hbox{\rm d}x_{1}}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}x_{1}$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}x_{1}$}}\ldots\intop_{-k}^{k}\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}x_{j-1}}{\hbox{\rm d}x_{j-1}}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}x_{j-1}$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}x_{j-1}$}}\intop_{-k}^{k}\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}x_{j+1}}{\hbox{\rm d}x_{j+1}}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}x_{j+1}$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}x_{j+1}$}}\ldots\intop_{-k}^{k}\left[\bar{f}(x)e^{-ix\cdot\omega}\right]_{x_{j}=-k}^{x_{j}=k}\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}x_{n}}{\hbox{\rm d}x_{n}}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}x_{n}$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}x_{n}$}}.

Then, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{k}\left(\partial_{j}f\right)

\displaystyle=i\omega_{j}\mathcal{F}_{k}\left(f\right)+\Delta_{jk}f\quad\forall j=1,\ldots,n

(6.1)

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{k}\left(\partial^{\alpha}f\right)

\displaystyle=\left(i\omega\right)^{\alpha}\mathcal{F}_{k}\left(f\right)+\sum_{p=0}^{|\alpha|-1}(i\omega)^{\alpha-\beta_{p}}\Delta_{j_{p}k}(\partial^{\beta_{p+1}}f).

(6.2)

In particular, if

f\left(x_{1},\ldots,x_{j-1},k,x_{j+1}\right)=f\left(x_{1},\ldots,x_{j-1},-k,x_{j+1}\right)=0\quad\forall x\in K,

(6.3)

then

\mathcal{F}_{k}\left(\partial_{j}f\right)=i\omega_{j}\mathcal{F}_{k}\left(f\right).

(ix)

\frac{\partial}{\partial\omega_{j}}\mathcal{F}_{k}\left(f\right)=-i\mathcal{F}_{k}\left(x_{j}f\right)

for all
j=1,\ldots,n
.

(x)

If
f\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{D}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{D}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{D}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{D}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GD}(K)
or
g\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{D}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{D}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{D}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{D}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GD}(K)
, then
\mathcal{F}_{k}\left(f\ast g\right)=\mathcal{F}_{k}\left(f\right)\mathcal{F}_{k}\left(g\right)
.
Therefore, if
f\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{D}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{D}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{D}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{D}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GD}(\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{n})
and
g\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{D}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{D}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{D}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{D}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GD}(\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{n})
, then

\mathcal{F}\left(f\ast g\right)=\mathcal{F}\left(f\right)\mathcal{F}\left(g\right)
.

(xi)

\mathcal{F}_{k}\left(s\odot g\right)=s\diamond\mathcal{F}_{\frac{k}{s}}\left(g\right)

for all invertible
s\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}
such that
\frac{k}{s}
is infinite,

g|_{K}\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GC}^{\infty}(K)
and
g|_{K/s}\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GC}^{\infty}(K/s)
.

Proof.

Properties
(i)
-
(v)
can be proved like in
the case of rapidly decreasing smooth functions. For
(vi)
,
we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{k}\left(s\oplus f\right)\left(\omega\right)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{F}_{k}\left(f\left(x-s\right)\right)\left(\omega\right)=\intop_{K}f\left(x-s\right)e^{-ix\cdot\omega}\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}x}{\hbox{\rm d}x}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}x$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}x$}}=

\displaystyle=\intop_{-k}^{k}\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}x_{1}}{\hbox{\rm d}x_{1}}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}x_{1}$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}x_{1}$}}\ldots\intop_{-k}^{k}f\left(x-s\right)e^{-ix\cdotp\omega}\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}x_{n}}{\hbox{\rm d}x_{n}}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}x_{n}$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}x_{n}$}}.

Considering the change of variable
x-s=u
we have

\mathcal{F}_{k}\left(s\oplus f\right)\left(\omega\right)=e^{-is\cdot\omega}\intop_{-k-s_{1}}^{k-s_{1}}\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}u_{1}}{\hbox{\rm d}u_{1}}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}u_{1}$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}u_{1}$}}\ldots\intop_{-k-s_{n}}^{k-s_{n}}f\left(u\right)e^{-iu\cdotp\omega}\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}u_{n}}{\hbox{\rm d}u_{n}}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}u_{n}$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}u_{n}$}}.

Finally, considering that
k>h
and
s\in[-k+h,k-h]^{n}
we have

k-s_{i}\geq h
,
-h\geq-k-s_{i}
and
k+s_{i}\geq h
for all
i=1,\ldots,n
,
so that

\displaystyle\intop_{-k-s_{1}}^{k-s_{1}}\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}u_{1}}{\hbox{\rm d}u_{1}}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}u_{1}$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}u_{1}$}}\ldots\intop_{-k-s_{n}}^{k-s_{n}}f\left(u\right)e^{-iu\cdotp\omega}\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}u_{n}}{\hbox{\rm d}u_{n}}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}u_{n}$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}u_{n}$}}

\displaystyle=\intop_{-h}^{h}\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}u_{1}}{\hbox{\rm d}u_{1}}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}u_{1}$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}u_{1}$}}\ldots\intop_{-h}^{h}f\left(u\right)e^{-iu\cdotp\omega}\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}u_{n}}{\hbox{\rm d}u_{n}}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}u_{n}$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}u_{n}$}}=

\displaystyle=\intop_{-k}^{k}\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}u_{1}}{\hbox{\rm d}u_{1}}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}u_{1}$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}u_{1}$}}\ldots\intop_{-k}^{k}f\left(u\right)e^{-iu\cdotp\omega}\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}u_{n}}{\hbox{\rm d}u_{n}}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}u_{n}$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}u_{n}$}}

from Def.
40
since
f\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{D}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{D}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{D}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{D}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GD}([-h,h]^{n})
.

(vii)
is immediate from the Def.
50
.

To prove
(viii)
, using integration by parts formula, we
have

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{k}\left(\partial_{j}f\right)\left(\omega\right)

\displaystyle=\intop_{K}\partial_{j}f\left(x\right)e^{-ix\cdot\omega}\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}x}{\hbox{\rm d}x}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}x$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}x$}}=\intop_{-k}^{k}\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}x_{1}}{\hbox{\rm d}x_{1}}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}x_{1}$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}x_{1}$}}\ldots\intop_{-k}^{k}\partial_{j}f\left(x\right)e^{-ix\cdotp\omega}\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}x_{n}}{\hbox{\rm d}x_{n}}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}x_{n}$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}x_{n}$}}=

\displaystyle=-\intop_{-k}^{k}\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}x_{1}}{\hbox{\rm d}x_{1}}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}x_{1}$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}x_{1}$}}\ldots\intop_{-k}^{k}f\left(x\right)\left(-i\omega_{j}\right)e^{-ix\cdotp\omega}\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}x_{n}}{\hbox{\rm d}x_{n}}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}x_{n}$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}x_{n}$}}+

\displaystyle\phantom{=}+\intop_{-k}^{k}\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}x_{1}}{\hbox{\rm d}x_{1}}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}x_{1}$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}x_{1}$}}\ldots\intop_{-k}^{k}\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}x_{j-1}}{\hbox{\rm d}x_{j-1}}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}x_{j-1}$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}x_{j-1}$}}\intop_{-k}^{k}\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}x_{j+1}}{\hbox{\rm d}x_{j+1}}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}x_{j+1}$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}x_{j+1}$}}\ldots\intop_{-k}^{k}\left[f(x)e^{-ix\cdot\omega}\right]_{x_{j}=-k}^{x_{j}=k}\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}x_{n}}{\hbox{\rm d}x_{n}}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}x_{n}$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}x_{n}$}}=

\displaystyle=i\omega_{j}\mathcal{F}_{k}\left(f\right)\left(\omega\right)+\Delta_{jk}f(\omega).

Therefore, by applying this formula with
\partial_{p}f
instead
of
f
, we obtain

\mathcal{F}_{k}\left(\partial_{j}\partial_{p}f\right)(\omega)=-\omega_{j}\omega_{p}\mathcal{F}_{k}(f)(\omega)+i\omega_{j}\Delta_{pk}\left(f\right)(\omega)+\Delta_{jk}\left(\partial_{p}f\right)(\omega).

Proceeding similarly by induction on
|\alpha|
, we can prove the
general claim.

To prove
(ix)
, we use Thm.
18
.
(viii)
,
i.e. derivation under the integral sign:

\displaystyle\frac{\partial}{\partial\omega_{j}}\mathcal{F}_{k}\left(f\right)\left(\omega\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{\partial}{\partial\omega_{j}}\left(\intop_{-k}^{k}\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}x_{1}}{\hbox{\rm d}x_{1}}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}x_{1}$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}x_{1}$}}\ldots\intop_{-k}^{k}f\left(x\right)e^{-ix\cdotp\omega}\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}x_{n}}{\hbox{\rm d}x_{n}}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}x_{n}$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}x_{n}$}}\right)=

\displaystyle=\intop_{-k}^{k}\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}x_{1}}{\hbox{\rm d}x_{1}}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}x_{1}$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}x_{1}$}}\ldots\intop_{-k}^{k}\frac{\partial}{\partial\omega_{j}}\left(f\left(x\right)e^{-ix\cdotp\omega}\right)\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}x_{n}}{\hbox{\rm d}x_{n}}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}x_{n}$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}x_{n}$}}=

\displaystyle=\intop_{-k}^{k}\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}x_{1}}{\hbox{\rm d}x_{1}}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}x_{1}$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}x_{1}$}}\ldots\intop_{-k}^{k}-ix_{j}f\left(x\right)e^{-ix\cdotp\omega}\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}x_{n}}{\hbox{\rm d}x_{n}}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}x_{n}$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}x_{n}$}}=

\displaystyle=-i\mathcal{F}_{k}\left(x_{j}f\right)\left(\omega\right).

(x)
:

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{k}\left(\left(f\ast g\right)\right)\left(\omega\right)

\displaystyle=\intop_{K}e^{-ix\omega}\left(f\ast g\right)\left(x\right)\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}x}{\hbox{\rm d}x}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}x$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}x$}}=

\displaystyle=\intop_{K}e^{-ix\omega}\intop_{K}f\left(y\right)g\left(x-y\right)\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}y}{\hbox{\rm d}y}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}y$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}y$}}\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}x}{\hbox{\rm d}x}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}x$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}x$}}.

Considering the change of variable
x-y=t
and using Fubini’s theorem,
we have

\displaystyle\intop_{K}e^{-i\left(t+y\right)\omega}\intop_{K}f\left(y\right)g\left(t\right)\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}y}{\hbox{\rm d}y}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}y$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}y$}}\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}t}{\hbox{\rm d}t}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}t$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}t$}}

\displaystyle=\intop_{K}e^{-iy\omega}f\left(y\right)\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}y}{\hbox{\rm d}y}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}y$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}y$}}\intop_{K}e^{-it\omega}g\left(t\right)\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}t}{\hbox{\rm d}t}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}t$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}t$}}=

\displaystyle=\mathcal{F}_{k}\left(f\right)\left(\omega\right)\mathcal{F}_{k}\left(g\right)\left(\omega\right).

Finally, we prove
(xi)
:

\mathcal{F}_{k}\left(s\odot g\right)\left(\omega\right)=\mathcal{F}_{k}\left(\frac{1}{s^{n}}g\left(\frac{x}{s}\right)\right)\left(\omega\right)=\intop_{K}e^{-ix\cdot\omega}g\left(\frac{x}{s}\right)\,\frac{\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}x}{\hbox{\rm d}x}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}x$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}x$}}}{s^{n}}.

Considering the change of variable
\frac{x}{s}=y
we have

\displaystyle\intop_{K}e^{-ix\cdot\omega}g\left(\frac{x}{s}\right)\,\frac{\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}x}{\hbox{\rm d}x}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}x$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}x$}}}{s^{n}}

\displaystyle=\intop_{-k/s}^{k/s}\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}y_{1}}{\hbox{\rm d}y_{1}}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}y_{1}$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}y_{1}$}}\ldots\intop_{-k/s}^{k/s}g\left(y\right)e^{-isy\cdotp\omega}\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}y_{n}}{\hbox{\rm d}y_{n}}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}y_{n}$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}y_{n}$}}=

\displaystyle=\intop_{K/s}g\left(y\right)e^{-iy\cdotp s\omega}\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}y}{\hbox{\rm d}y}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}y$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}y$}}=\mathcal{F}_{k/s}\left(g\right)\left(s\omega\right)=

\displaystyle=\left[s\diamond\mathcal{F}_{k/s}\left(g\right)\right](\omega).

∎

We will see in Sec.
9
that the
additional term in (
6.2
) plays an important role in
finding
non tempered
solutions of differential equations (like
the exponentials of the trivial ODE
y^{\prime}=y
). We also note that condition
(
6.3
) is clearly weaker than asking
f
compactly supported.
For example, setting

l_{j}(x):=\frac{1}{2k}\left[f\left(x\right)|_{x_{j}=k}-f\left(x\right)|_{x_{j}=-k}\right]\cdot(x_{j}+k)+f\left(x\right)|_{x_{j}=-k},

then
\bar{f}:=f-l_{j}
satisfies (
6.3
).

7.
The inverse hyperfinite Fourier
transform

We naturally define the inverse HFT as follows:

Definition 58
.

Let
f\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GC}^{\infty}\left(K\right)
, the
inverse
HFT is

\mathcal{F}_{k}^{-1}\left(f\right)(x):=\frac{1}{\left(2\pi\right)^{n}}\intop_{K}f\left(\omega\right)e^{ix\cdot\omega}\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\omega}{\hbox{\rm d}\omega}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\omega$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\omega$}}

(7.1)

for all
x\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}
. As we proved in Thm.
51
,
we have
\mathcal{F}_{k}^{-1}:\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GC}^{\infty}\left(K\right)\longrightarrow\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GC}^{\infty}\left(\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{n}\right)
.
We immediately note that the notation of the inverse function
\mathcal{F}_{k}^{-1}

is an abuse of language because the codomain of
\mathcal{F}_{k}

is larger than the domain of
\mathcal{F}_{k}^{-1}
(and vice versa).
When dealing with inversion properties, it is hence better to think
at

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{k}|_{K}

\displaystyle:=(-)|_{K}\circ\mathcal{F}_{k}:\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GC}^{\infty}\left(K\right)\longrightarrow\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GC}^{\infty}\left(K\right)

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{k}^{-1}|_{K}

\displaystyle:=(-)|_{K}\circ\mathcal{F}_{k}^{-1}:\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GC}^{\infty}\left(K\right)\longrightarrow\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GC}^{\infty}\left(K\right).

We will see in Sec.
9
that lacking
this precision can easily lead to inconsistencies.

Note that

\left(2\pi\right)^{n}\mathcal{F}_{k}^{-1}(f)=\mathcal{F}_{k}\left(-1\diamond f\right)=-1\diamond\mathcal{F}_{k}(f),

(7.2)

where
-1\diamond
denotes the reflection
\left(-1\diamond g\right)(x):=g(-x)
.

7.1.
The Fourier inversion theorem

Our main goal is clearly to investigate the relationship between HFT
and its inverse HFT, i.e. to prove the Fourier inversion theorem
for the HFT. Three important results used in the classical proof of
the Fourier inversion theorem are: the application of approximate
identities for convolution defined by Gaussian like functions (see

[
43
, Lem. 4.3]
for a similar result), Lebesgue dominated
converge theorem (we can replace it with Thm.
35
),
and the translation property of FT. In our setting, the last property
corresponds to Thm.
57
.
(vi)
, which
works only for compactly supported GSF. A first idea could hence to
avoid proving the inversion theorem firstly at the origin and then
employing the translation property, but to prove it directly at an
arbitrary interior point
y\in\mathring{K}
using approximate identities
obtained by mollification of a Gaussian function. Unfortunately, this
idea does not work: in fact, if
g(z):=(2\pi)^{-n/2}e^{-\frac{z^{2}}{2}}
,
then our approximate identity would be the mollification
G_{p}:=\frac{1}{p}\odot g
,
where we think
p\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}
,
p\to+\infty
. We would also need a
function
g_{p}
such that
\mathcal{F}\left[g_{p}(-,y)\right](x)=(2\pi)^{n/2}G_{p}(y-x)
,
i.e.
g_{p}(-,y):=e^{iy\cdot\left(-\right)}\cdot\left(t_{p}\diamond g\right)
.
The first problem is that
\text{supp}\left(g_{p}(-,y)\right)\subseteq\overline{B_{p\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}^{-1}}(0)}\uparrow\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}

as
p\to+\infty
. In an integral of the type
\int_{K}\mathcal{F}_{k}(f)(\omega)g_{p}(\omega,y)\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\omega}{\hbox{\rm d}\omega}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\omega$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\omega$}}

we would therefore need
k

non

\rho
-moderate (see below,
Def.
62
) to contain all the support of
g_{p}(-,y)
.
On the other hand, we would also need
\mathchoice{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\raisebox{-0.39864pt}{$\rho\hskip-5.49028pt$}$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\raisebox{-0.39864pt}{$\rho\hskip-5.49028pt$}$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\raisebox{-0.39864pt}{$\rho\hskip-5.49028pt$}$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\raisebox{-0.39864pt}{$\rho\hskip-5.49028pt$}$}}}\lim_{p\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}\,}}{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}\lim_{p\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}\,}}{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}\lim_{p\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}\,}}{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}\lim_{p\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}\,}}g_{p}(\omega,y)=\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{n}}e^{iy\cdot\omega}

, and it is not hard to prove that
\left|g_{p}(\omega,y)-\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{n}}e^{iy\cdot\omega}\right|\leq\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}^{q}

if
p\geq C_{2}k\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}^{-q/2}
for some
C_{2}\in\mathbb{R}_{>0}
, and
this implies that
k
must be
\rho
-moderate.

The idea for a different proof starts from the following calculations
(for
n=1
):

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{k}(f)(\omega)

\displaystyle=\int_{K}f(x)e^{-ix\omega}\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}x}{\hbox{\rm d}x}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}x$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}x$}}=\left[\int_{K_{\varepsilon}}f_{\varepsilon}(x)e^{-ix\omega_{\varepsilon}}\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}x}{\hbox{\rm d}x}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}x$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}x$}}\right]

\displaystyle=\left[\int_{\mathbb{R}}\chi_{K_{\varepsilon}}(x)f_{\varepsilon}(x)e^{-ix\omega_{\varepsilon}}\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}x}{\hbox{\rm d}x}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}x$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}x$}}\right]=\left[\hat{\mathcal{F}}\left(\chi_{K_{\varepsilon}}f_{\varepsilon}\right)(\omega_{\varepsilon})\right],

where
\chi_{K_{\varepsilon}}
is the characteristic function of
K_{\varepsilon}:=[-k_{\varepsilon},k_{\varepsilon}]
,
and
\hat{\mathcal{F}}
is the classical Fourier transform. Thereby,
if we take
another
positive infinite number
h=[h_{\varepsilon}]\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}

and set
H:=[-h,h]
,
H_{\varepsilon}:=[-h_{\varepsilon},h_{\varepsilon}]
, then

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{h}^{-1}\left(\mathcal{F}_{k}(f)\right)(y)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{H}e^{iy\omega}\left[\hat{\mathcal{F}}\left(\chi_{K_{\varepsilon}}f_{\varepsilon}\right)(\omega)\right]\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\omega}{\hbox{\rm d}\omega}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\omega$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\omega$}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2\pi}\left[\int_{\mathbb{R}}e^{iy_{\varepsilon}\omega}\chi_{H_{\varepsilon}}(\omega)\hat{\mathcal{F}}\left(\chi_{K_{\varepsilon}}f_{\varepsilon}\right)(\omega)\right]

\displaystyle=\left[\hat{\mathcal{F}}^{-1}\left(\chi_{H_{\varepsilon}}\cdot\hat{\mathcal{F}}\left(\chi_{K_{\varepsilon}}f_{\varepsilon}\right)\right)(y_{\varepsilon})\right]

\displaystyle=\left[\left(\hat{\mathcal{F}}^{-1}(\chi_{H_{\varepsilon}})*\chi_{K_{\varepsilon}}f_{\varepsilon}\right)(y_{\varepsilon})\right].

We now compute

\displaystyle\hat{\mathcal{F}}^{-1}(\chi_{H_{\varepsilon}})(z)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{\mathbb{R}}e^{iz\omega}\chi_{H_{\varepsilon}}(\omega)\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\omega}{\hbox{\rm d}\omega}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\omega$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\omega$}}=\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{-h_{\varepsilon}}^{h_{\varepsilon}}e^{iz\omega}\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\omega}{\hbox{\rm d}\omega}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\omega$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\omega$}}=\frac{1}{\pi}h_{\varepsilon}S(h_{\varepsilon}z),

where
S(x)=\frac{1}{2}\int_{-1}^{1}\cos(xt)\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}t}{\hbox{\rm d}t}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}t$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}t$}}
is the smooth
extension of
\frac{\sin(x)}{x}
at
x=0
. Therefore, we can write

\mathcal{F}_{h}^{-1}\left(\mathcal{F}_{k}(f)\right)(y)=\int_{K}\frac{h}{\pi}S(h(y-x))f(x)\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}x}{\hbox{\rm d}x}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}x$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}x$}}.

(7.3)

For
n\geq 1
, we similarly have

\hat{\mathcal{F}}^{-1}(\chi_{H_{\varepsilon}})(z)=\frac{1}{\pi^{n}}h_{1\varepsilon}S(z_{1}h_{1\varepsilon})\cdot\displaystyle\mathop{\ldots\ldots\,}^{n}\cdot h_{n\varepsilon}S(z_{n}h_{n\varepsilon})=:\delta_{h_{\varepsilon}}^{n}(z),

(7.4)

and

\mathcal{F}_{h}^{-1}\left(\mathcal{F}_{k}(f)\right)(y)=\int_{K}\delta_{h}^{n}(y-x)\cdot f(x)\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}x}{\hbox{\rm d}x}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}x$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}x$}}.

(7.5)

We call the GSF (
h
is an infinite number)
\delta_{h}^{n}

Dirichlet
delta function
(recall that the delta sequence
\left(\delta_{n}^{n}\right)_{h\in\mathbb{N}}

converges to
\delta
in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}
, see e.g.
[
6
]
).
In fact, these calculations lead us to consider the so-called Dirichlet
sifting theorem

\lim_{h\to+\infty}\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}\delta_{h}^{1}(x)f(x)=f(0),

(7.6)

which holds for
f\in\mathcal{C}^{1}(\mathbb{R})
such that
f^{\prime}
is bounded
(see e.g.
[
38
,
6
]
). Formula (
7.6
)
also justifies why we considered another infinite number
h
; moreover,
in the following proof, we will see that the use of the functionally
compact set
K
instead of
\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}
allows us
to avoid any limitation on the GSF
f
.

We first need the following results:

Lemma 59
.

For all sharply interior point
y\in\mathring{K}
,
we have

\lim_{h\to+\infty}\int_{K}\delta_{h}^{n}(y-x)\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}x}{\hbox{\rm d}x}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}x$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}x$}}=1.

Here, the limit is in the sharp topology, i.e.

\forall q\in\mathbb{N}\,\exists\bar{h}\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}\,\forall h\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}_{\geq\bar{h}}:\ \left|\int_{K}\delta_{h}^{n}(y-x)\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}x}{\hbox{\rm d}x}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}x$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}x$}}-1\right|\leq\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}^{q}.

Proof.

We actually prove the case
n=1
, since
n>1
is similar. From
y=[y_{\varepsilon}]\in\mathring{K}=(-k,k)
,
we can take the representative
(y_{\varepsilon})
so that
-k_{\varepsilon}<y_{\varepsilon}<k_{\varepsilon}

for all
\varepsilon
. We have
\int_{K}\delta_{h}^{1}(y-x)\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}x}{\hbox{\rm d}x}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}x$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}x$}}=\left[\frac{h_{\varepsilon}}{\pi}\int_{-k_{\varepsilon}}^{k_{\varepsilon}}S\left(h_{\varepsilon}(y_{\varepsilon}-x)\right)\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}x}{\hbox{\rm d}x}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}x$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}x$}}\right]
.
With the change of variables
x^{\prime}=h_{\varepsilon}(y_{\varepsilon}-x)
, we get

\displaystyle\frac{h_{\varepsilon}}{\pi}\int_{-k_{\varepsilon}}^{k_{\varepsilon}}S\left(h_{\varepsilon}(y_{\varepsilon}-x)\right)\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}x}{\hbox{\rm d}x}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}x$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}x$}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\pi}\int_{h_{\varepsilon}y_{\varepsilon}-h_{\varepsilon}k_{\varepsilon}}^{h_{\varepsilon}y_{\varepsilon}+h_{\varepsilon}k_{\varepsilon}}S

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\pi}\left(\int_{h_{\varepsilon}y_{\varepsilon}-h_{\varepsilon}k_{\varepsilon}}^{-\infty}S+\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}S+\int_{+\infty}^{h_{\varepsilon}y_{\varepsilon}+h_{\varepsilon}k_{\varepsilon}}S\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\pi}\left(\int_{h_{\varepsilon}y_{\varepsilon}-h_{\varepsilon}k_{\varepsilon}}^{-\infty}S+\pi+\int_{+\infty}^{h_{\varepsilon}y_{\varepsilon}+h_{\varepsilon}k_{\varepsilon}}S\right).

Note that
h_{\varepsilon}y_{\varepsilon}+h_{\varepsilon}k_{\varepsilon}\geq 0
and
h_{\varepsilon}y_{\varepsilon}-h_{\varepsilon}k_{\varepsilon}\leq 0

because
-k_{\varepsilon}\leq y_{\varepsilon}\leq k_{\varepsilon}
. In general, if
0<a\leq b

or
a\leq b<0
, we have

\displaystyle\left|\int_{a}^{b}S\right|

\displaystyle=\left|\int_{a}^{b}\frac{\sin x}{x}\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}x}{\hbox{\rm d}x}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}x$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}x$}}\right|=\left|-\frac{\cos y}{y}\right|_{a}^{b}+\left.\int_{a}^{b}\frac{\cos y}{y^{2}}\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}y}{\hbox{\rm d}y}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}y$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}y$}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{|b|}+\frac{1}{|a|}+\int_{a}^{b}\frac{\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}y}{\hbox{\rm d}y}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}y$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}y$}}}{y^{2}}=\frac{2}{|a|}+\frac{2}{|b|}.

In our cases, this yields

\displaystyle\left|\int_{h_{\varepsilon}y_{\varepsilon}+h_{\varepsilon}k_{\varepsilon}}^{+\infty}S\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2}{\left|h_{\varepsilon}y_{\varepsilon}+h_{\varepsilon}k_{\varepsilon}\right|}

\displaystyle\left|\int_{-\infty}^{h_{\varepsilon}y_{\varepsilon}-h_{\varepsilon}k_{\varepsilon}}S\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2}{\left|h_{\varepsilon}y_{\varepsilon}-h_{\varepsilon}k_{\varepsilon}\right|}

(recall that
-k_{\varepsilon}\leq y_{\varepsilon}\leq k_{\varepsilon}
). Therefore

\left|\int_{K}\delta_{h}^{1}(y-x)\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}x}{\hbox{\rm d}x}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}x$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}x$}}\right|\leq 1+\frac{2}{\left|hy+hk\right|}+\frac{2}{\left|hy-hk\right|}\to 1

as
h\to+\infty
because
-k<y<k
.
∎

Now, we have to deal with estimations of the convolution
(
7.5
) on the “tails”, i.e. arbitrarily
near
y
:

Lemma 60
.

Let
K\subseteq X\subseteq\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
and

f\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GC}^{\infty}(X).
Then for all
\delta\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}
such that
B_{\delta}(y)\subseteq K
,
we have:

(i)

\lim_{h\to+\infty}\int_{-k}^{y-\delta}\delta_{h}^{n}(y-x)\cdot f(x)\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}x}{\hbox{\rm d}x}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}x$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}x$}}=0=\lim_{h\to+\infty}\int_{y+\delta}^{k}\delta_{h}^{n}(y-x)\cdot f(x)\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}x}{\hbox{\rm d}x}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}x$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}x$}}
.

(ii)

\lim_{h\to+\infty}\left(\mathcal{F}_{h}^{-1}\left(\mathcal{F}_{k}(f)\right)(y)-\int_{y-\delta}^{y+\delta}\delta_{h}^{n}(y-x)\cdot f(x)\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}x}{\hbox{\rm d}x}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}x$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}x$}}\right)=0
.

As above, the limits are in the sharp topology.

Proof.

For
y<a\leq b\leq k
or
-k\leq a\leq b<y
, we first consider

\displaystyle\int_{a}^{b}\delta_{h}^{1}(y

\displaystyle-x)f(x)\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}x}{\hbox{\rm d}x}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}x$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}x$}}=\int_{a}^{b}\frac{1}{\pi}\frac{\sin(h(y-x))}{y-x}f(x)\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}x}{\hbox{\rm d}x}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}x$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}x$}}=

\displaystyle=-\int_{hy-ha}^{hy-hb}\frac{1}{\pi}\frac{\sin z}{z}f\left(y-\frac{z}{h}\right)\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}z}{\hbox{\rm d}z}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}z$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}z$}}=

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\pi}\left(\left[-\frac{\cos z}{z}f\left(y-\frac{z}{h}\right)\right]_{hy-hb}^{hy-ha}+\int_{hy-hb}^{hy-ha}\cos(z)\frac{\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}}{\hbox{\rm d}}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}$}}}{\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}z}{\hbox{\rm d}z}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}z$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}z$}}}\left[\frac{f\left(y-\frac{z}{h}\right)}{z}\right]\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}z}{\hbox{\rm d}z}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}z$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}z$}}\right).

(7.7)

The first summand in (
7.7
) yields

\displaystyle\left|\left[-\frac{\cos z}{z}f\left(y-\frac{z}{h}\right)\right]_{hy-hb}^{hy-ha}\right|

\displaystyle=\left|-\cos(hy-ha)\frac{f(a)}{hy-ha}+\cos(hy-hb)\frac{f(b)}{hy-hb}\right|\leq

\displaystyle\leq\frac{|f(a)|}{h}\cdot\frac{1}{|y-a|}+\frac{|f(b)|}{h}\cdot\frac{1}{|y-b|}

(7.8)

The second summand in (
7.7
) yields

\displaystyle\int_{hy-hb}^{hy-ha}\cos(z)\frac{\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}}{\hbox{\rm d}}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}$}}}{\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}z}{\hbox{\rm d}z}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}z$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}z$}}}\left[\frac{f\left(y-\frac{z}{h}\right)}{z}\right]\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}z}{\hbox{\rm d}z}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}z$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}z$}}

\displaystyle=-\int_{hy-hb}^{hy-ha}\cos(z)\frac{f^{\prime}\left(y-\frac{z}{h}\right)}{hz}\ \mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}z}{\hbox{\rm d}z}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}z$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}z$}}

(7.9)

\displaystyle\phantom{=}-\int_{hy-hb}^{hy-ha}\cos(z)\frac{f\left(y-\frac{z}{h}\right)}{z^{2}}\ \mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}z}{\hbox{\rm d}z}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}z$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}z$}}.

(7.10)

If
hy-hb\leq z\leq hy-ha
, then
-k\leq a\leq y-\frac{z}{h}\leq b\leq k
,
so that
\left|f^{\prime}\left(y-\frac{z}{h}\right)\right|\leq\max_{x\in K}\left|f^{\prime}(x)\right|=:M_{1}\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}

(note that this step would not be so trivial if we had to let
k\to+\infty
).
Thereby, from the mean value theorem applied to (
7.9
)

\left|\int_{hy-hb}^{hy-ha}\cos(z)\frac{f^{\prime}\left(y-\frac{z}{h}\right)}{hz}\ \mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}z}{\hbox{\rm d}z}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}z$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}z$}}\right|\leq(b-a)\cdot\frac{M_{1}}{\left|\zeta\right|}

for some
\zeta\in[hy-hb,hy-ha]
(note that
a\leq b
implies
hy-ha\geq hy-hb
).
In the first case we assumed, i.e.
y<a\leq b\leq k
, we have
hy-ha<0
,
so that
|\zeta|=-\zeta\geq ha-hy
, and hence
(b-a)\cdot\frac{M_{1}}{\left|\zeta\right|}\leq(b-a)\cdot\frac{M_{1}}{ha-hy}
.
In the second case
-k\leq a\leq b<y
, we have
|\zeta|=\zeta\geq hy-hb
,
and hence
(b-a)\cdot\frac{M_{1}}{\left|\zeta\right|}\leq(b-a)\cdot\frac{M_{1}}{hy-hb}
.
The last term in (
7.10
) can be estimated as

\left|\int_{hy-hb}^{hy-ha}\cos(z)\frac{f\left(y-\frac{z}{h}\right)}{z^{2}}\ \mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}z}{\hbox{\rm d}z}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}z$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}z$}}\right|\leq M_{0}\int_{hy-hb}^{hy-ha}\frac{\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}z}{\hbox{\rm d}z}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}z$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}z$}}}{z^{2}}=M_{0}\left(\frac{1}{hy-hb}-\frac{1}{hy-ha}\right),

where
M_{0}:=\max_{x\in K}\left|f(x)\right|\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}
. Applying these
estimates with
a=-k
and
b=y-\delta
(so that the second case
holds), we get

\displaystyle\left|\int_{-k}^{y-\delta}\delta_{h}^{n}(y-x)\cdot f(x)\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}x}{\hbox{\rm d}x}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}x$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}x$}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{|f(-k)|}{h}\cdot\frac{1}{|y+k|}+\frac{|f(y-\delta)|}{h}\cdot\frac{1}{\delta}+\frac{(y-\delta+k)M_{1}}{h\delta}+

\displaystyle\phantom{\leq}+\frac{M_{0}}{h}\left(\frac{1}{y+k}-\frac{1}{\delta}\right)\leq

\displaystyle\leq 2\frac{M_{0}}{h}\frac{1}{|y+k|}+\frac{(y-\delta+k)M_{1}}{h\delta}.

For
h\to+\infty
, this proves the first part of
(i)
.
Once again, note that if
h=k\to+\infty
, in general the term
\frac{|f(-k)|}{k}\not\to 0
;
it is hence important that
k
is fixed and only
h\to+\infty
.
Similarly, we can estimate the other integral in
(i)

for
a=y+\delta
and
b=k
(the first case holds) obtaining

\left|\int_{y+\delta}^{k}\delta_{h}^{n}(y-x)\cdot f(x)\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}x}{\hbox{\rm d}x}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}x$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}x$}}\right|\leq 2\frac{M_{0}}{h}\frac{1}{|y-k|}+\frac{(k-y-\delta)M_{1}}{h\delta}\to 0

as
h\to+\infty
.

The claim
(ii)
is proved considering (
7.5
)
and
(i)
.
∎

Finally, we have the Fourier inversion theorem:

Theorem 61
.

Let
K\subseteq X\subseteq\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
and
f\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GC}^{\infty}(X)
.
Then for all sharply interior
y\in\mathring{K}
, we have

\lim_{h\to+\infty}\mathcal{F}_{h}^{-1}\left(\mathcal{F}_{k}(f)\right)(y)=\lim_{h\to+\infty}\mathcal{F}_{h}\left(\mathcal{F}_{k}^{-1}(f)\right)(y)=f(y).

(7.11)

Proof.

To link the integrals of the previous Lem.
60

with
f(y)
, we use the Fermat-Reyes Thm.
14
:
for any
\delta\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}
sufficiently small such that
B_{\delta}(y)\subseteq K\subseteq X
,
we can write
f(x)=f(y)+(y-x)f^{\prime}[y;y-x]
for all
x\in[y-\delta,y+\delta]
.
Therefore

\int_{y-\delta}^{y+\delta}\delta_{h}^{1}(y-x)\cdot f(x)\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}x}{\hbox{\rm d}x}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}x$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}x$}}=\int_{y-\delta}^{y+\delta}\delta_{h}^{1}(y-x)\cdot\left(f(y)+(y-x)f^{\prime}[y;y-x]\right)\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}x}{\hbox{\rm d}x}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}x$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}x$}}\\
=f(y)\int_{y-\delta}^{y+\delta}\delta_{h}^{1}(y-x)\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}x}{\hbox{\rm d}x}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}x$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}x$}}+\frac{1}{\pi}\int_{y-\delta}^{y+\delta}\sin(h(y-x))f^{\prime}[y;y-x]\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}x}{\hbox{\rm d}x}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}x$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}x$}}.

Thereby

\left|\int_{y-\delta}^{y+\delta}\delta_{h}^{1}(y-x)\cdot f(x)\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}x}{\hbox{\rm d}x}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}x$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}x$}}-f(y)\int_{y-\delta}^{y+\delta}\delta_{h}^{1}(y-x)\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}x}{\hbox{\rm d}x}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}x$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}x$}}\right|\leq\frac{1}{\pi}2\delta M_{1\delta},

where
M_{1\delta}:=\max_{x\in[y-\delta,y+\delta]}|f^{\prime}[x;y-x]|\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}
.
Considering (
7.5
), we have

\displaystyle\left|\mathcal{F}_{h}^{-1}\left(\mathcal{F}_{k}(f)\right)(y)-f(y)\right.

\displaystyle\left.\int_{-k}^{k}\delta_{h}^{1}(y-x)\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}x}{\hbox{\rm d}x}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}x$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}x$}}\right|\leq\frac{1}{\pi}2\delta M_{1\delta}+

\displaystyle\left|\int_{-k}^{y-\delta}\delta_{h}^{1}(y-x)\cdot f(x)\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}x}{\hbox{\rm d}x}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}x$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}x$}}+\int_{y+\delta}^{k}\delta_{h}^{1}(y-x)\cdot f(x)\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}x}{\hbox{\rm d}x}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}x$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}x$}}\right.

\displaystyle-\left.f(y)\int_{-k}^{y-\delta}\delta_{h}^{1}(y-x)\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}x}{\hbox{\rm d}x}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}x$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}x$}}-f(y)\int_{y+\delta}^{k}\delta_{h}^{1}(y-x)\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}x}{\hbox{\rm d}x}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}x$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}x$}}\right|.

Take the limit both for
h\to+\infty
and for
\delta\to 0^{+}
in
this inequality, considering that the left hand side does not depend
on
\delta
. Using Lem.
60
, we obtain

\lim_{h\to+\infty}\left|\mathcal{F}_{h}^{-1}\left(\mathcal{F}_{k}(f)\right)(y)-f(y)\int_{-k}^{k}\delta_{h}^{1}(y-x)\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}x}{\hbox{\rm d}x}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}x$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}x$}}\right|=0.

Finally, Lem.
59
yields
\lim_{h\to+\infty}f(y)\int_{-k}^{k}\delta_{h}^{1}(y-x)\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}x}{\hbox{\rm d}x}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}x$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}x$}}=f(y)

and this proves the first part of the claim. To prove the second equality
in (
7.11
), we use the general equalities

\displaystyle(2\pi)^{n}\mathcal{F}_{h}^{-1}(g)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{F}_{h}(-1\diamond g)\qquad\forall g\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GC}^{\infty}(H)

(7.12)

\displaystyle(2\pi)^{n}\mathcal{F}_{h}^{-1}(f)

\displaystyle=-1\diamond\mathcal{F}_{k}(f)\qquad\forall f\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GC}^{\infty}(K).

(7.13)

Applying (
7.12
) with
g=\mathcal{F}_{k}(f)|_{H}
, we get

(2\pi)^{n}\mathcal{F}_{h}^{-1}(\mathcal{F}_{k}(f))=\mathcal{F}_{h}(-1\diamond\mathcal{F}_{k}(f)).

(7.14)

Thereby, using (
7.13
) and (
7.14
), we get
(2\pi)^{n}\mathcal{F}_{h}(\mathcal{F}_{k}^{-1}(f))=\mathcal{F}_{h}((2\pi)^{n}\mathcal{F}_{k}^{-1}(f))=\mathcal{F}_{h}(-1\diamond\mathcal{F}_{k}(f))=(2\pi)^{n}\mathcal{F}_{h}^{-1}(\mathcal{F}_{k}(f))
.
∎

One way to summarize the meaning of this version of the
Fourier inversion theorem is as follows: If for a smooth function

g\in\mathcal{C}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})
, we want to define

\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}g(\omega)e^{ix\omega}\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\omega}{\hbox{\rm d}\omega}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\omega$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\omega$}}:=\lim_{h\to+\infty}\int_{-h}^{h}g(\omega)e^{ix\omega}\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\omega}{\hbox{\rm d}\omega}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\omega$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\omega$}},

we can assume some sufficiently strong behavior of
g
at
\pm\infty
,
e.g. that
g
is rapidly decreasing. On the one hand, this is only
a sufficient condition deeply linked to the limitations of the Lebesgue
integral, as the function
g(x)=\frac{\sin(x)}{x}
shows. On the
other hand, (
7.11
) shows that this type of limit exists (in
the sharp topology) for all the GSF of the form
g=\mathcal{F}_{k}(f)
,
where
f
is an
arbitrary
GSF and
k\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}
is
any
fixed infinite number
. Indeed, Thm.
61
is strongly related
to the Dirichlet delta, as (
7.5
) already
shows. In other words, a key idea of the present work is to consider
the HFT
\mathcal{F}_{h}^{-1}(f)
for arbitrary
f\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GC}^{\infty}(H)
, and
to take the limit for
h\to+\infty
only in the Fourier inversion
theorem.

We can also state the Fourier inversion theorem using a strong equivalence
relation instead of a limit. For this aim, we need the following notions:

Definition 62
.

If
\sigma
is a gauge
smaller
than
\rho
,
and we write
\sigma\leq\rho^{*}
, i.e. if

\exists R\in\mathbb{R}_{>0}\,\forall^{0}\varepsilon:\ \sigma_{\varepsilon}\leq\rho_{\varepsilon}^{R},

then we have
\mathbb{R}_{\rho}\subseteq\mathbb{R}_{\sigma}
and we can hence consider
the set of
\rho
-moderate numbers in

\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}
:

\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}_{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}_{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}_{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}_{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}:=\left\{[x_{\varepsilon}]\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}\mid(x_{\varepsilon})\in\mathbb{R}_{\rho}\right\}.

Let
\partial\rho:=[\rho_{\varepsilon}]\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}
denotes the generalized
number in
\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}
defined by the net
(\rho_{\varepsilon})
. If
x
,

y\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}
, we say that
x

is equal up to
\rho

to
y
, and we write
x=_{\rho}y
, if

\forall q\in\mathbb{N}:\ |x-y|\leq\partial\rho^{q}.

We say that
\sigma
is an
auxiliary gauge of
\rho
, and
we write
\sigma\ll\rho
, if

\exists Q\in\mathbb{N}\,\forall q\in\mathbb{N}\,\forall^{0}\varepsilon:\ \sigma_{\varepsilon}^{Q}\leq\rho_{\varepsilon}^{q}.

(7.15)

Finally, we say that
k\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}
is
\rho
-immoderate
,
and we write
k\gg\partial\rho^{-*}
if

\forall Q\in\mathbb{N}:\ k\geq\partial\rho^{-Q}.

Remark 63
.

(i)

Clearly,
\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}_{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}_{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}_{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}_{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}
is a subring of
\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}
,
but in general it is not isomorphic to
\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}
because the notion
of equality
\sim_{\sigma}
in
\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}
is generally stronger
than the one
\sim_{\rho}
in
\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}
. However, if
[x_{\varepsilon}]_{\sigma}\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}

and
[x_{\varepsilon}]_{\rho}\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}
denotes the equivalence classes generated
by the net
(x_{\varepsilon})\in\mathbb{R}_{\rho}
, then the map

\iota:[x_{\varepsilon}]_{\sigma}\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}_{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}_{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}_{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}_{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}\mapsto[x_{\varepsilon}]\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}

is surjective and “injective up to
\rho
”, i.e.
\iota(x)=\iota(y)

implies
x=_{\rho}y
. Similarly, we can define

f\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}_{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}_{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}_{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}_{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\mathcal{GC}^{\infty}(X,Y):\Leftrightarrow X\subseteq\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}_{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}_{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}_{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}_{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}},\ Y\subseteq\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}_{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}_{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}_{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}_{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}},\ \forall x\in X\,\forall\alpha\in\mathbb{N}^{n}:\ \partial^{\alpha}f\left(x\right)\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}_{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}_{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}_{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}_{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}},

and the map
j:\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}_{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}_{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}_{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}_{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\mathcal{GC}^{\infty}(X,Y)\longrightarrow\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GC}^{\infty}(\iota(X),\iota(Y))

defined by
j(f)(\iota(x)):=\iota(f(x))
is surjective and satisfies

j(f)=j(g)
if and only if
f(x)=_{\rho}g(x)
for all
x\in X
.

(ii)

\sigma_{1\varepsilon}:=\rho_{\varepsilon}^{1/\varepsilon}
,

\sigma_{2\varepsilon}:=\exp\left(-\frac{1}{\rho_{\varepsilon}}\right)
and
\sigma_{3\varepsilon}:=\exp\left(-\rho_{\varepsilon}^{-1/\varepsilon}\right)

are all auxiliary gauges of
\rho
,
k_{j}:=\sigma_{j}^{-1}
and

-\log k_{3}
are
\rho
-immoderate numbers. On the other hand,
if
\sigma
is an arbitrary gauge, and
\rho_{\varepsilon}:=-\log(\sigma_{\varepsilon})^{-1}
,
then
\sigma\ll\rho
.

Corollary 64
.

Let
\sigma\ll\rho
and
k\gg\partial\rho^{-*}
.
Let
f\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\mathcal{GC}^{\infty}(X)
, with
K\subseteq X
. Then for all
h\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}

sufficiently large, we have

\mathcal{F}_{h}^{-1}\left(\mathcal{F}_{k}(f)\right)(y)=_{\rho}\mathcal{F}_{h}\left(\mathcal{F}_{k}^{-1}(f)\right)(y)=_{\rho}f(y)

for all
y\in K\cap\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}_{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}_{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}_{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}_{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}
.

Proof.

From Thm.
61
(with
\sigma
in the play of
\rho
),
we have that for all
h\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}
sufficiently large

\left|\mathcal{F}_{h}^{-1}\left(\mathcal{F}_{k}(f)\right)(y)-f(y)\right|\leq\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\sigma}{\hbox{\rm d}\sigma}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\sigma$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\sigma$}}^{Q}\leq\partial\rho^{q}

for all
q\in\mathbb{N}
from (
7.15
). Note that
y\in K\cap\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}_{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}_{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}_{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}_{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}

and
k\gg\partial\rho^{-*}
imply
y\in\mathring{K}
(in the
\sigma
-sharp
topology).
∎

The following results allows one to have independence from
k
or
both
k
and
h
.

Corollary 65
.

If
f\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{D}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{D}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{D}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{D}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GD}(\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}})
, there exists
k\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}

infinite such that

\lim_{h\to+\infty}\mathcal{F}_{h}^{-1}\left(\mathcal{F}(f)\right)(y)=\lim_{h\to+\infty}\mathcal{F}_{h}\left(\mathcal{F}^{-1}(f)\right)(y)=f(y)

for all
y\in\mathring{K}
.

Corollary 66
.

Let
H\Subset_{\text{\rm f}}\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
and
f\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{D}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{D}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{D}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{D}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GD}(H)
.
Assume that

\exists C,b\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\,\forall x\in H\,\forall j\in\mathbb{N}:\ \left|\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}^{j}f}{\hbox{\rm d}^{j}f}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}^{j}f$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}^{j}f$}}(x)\right|\leq C\cdot b^{j}.

Then

\mathcal{F}^{-1}\left(\mathcal{F}(f)\right)(y)=\mathcal{F}\left(\mathcal{F}^{-1}(f)\right)(y)=f(y)\qquad\forall y\in H.

Proof.

See the Riemann-Lebesgue Lem.
52
, which guarantees
that also
\mathcal{F}(f)
is compactly supported.
∎

In the following result, we summarize some properties of Dirichlet
delta function:

Corollary 67
.

Let
K\subseteq X\subseteq\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
,

f\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GC}^{\infty}(X)
,
h\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}
be a positive infinite number, and
\delta\in(0,1]
.
Then, the following properties hold:

(i)

\delta_{h}^{n}=\delta_{n}^{1}\cdot\ldots\cdot\delta_{n}^{1}
;

(ii)

\lim_{h\to+\infty}\int_{-k}^{k}\delta_{h}^{n}(x)\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}x}{\hbox{\rm d}x}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}x$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}x$}}=1
;

(iii)

\lim_{h\to+\infty}\int_{-k}^{-\delta}\delta_{h}^{n}(x)\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}x}{\hbox{\rm d}x}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}x$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}x$}}=\lim_{h\to+\infty}\int_{\delta}^{k}\delta_{h}^{n}(x)\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}x}{\hbox{\rm d}x}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}x$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}x$}}=0
;

(iv)

\lim_{h\to+\infty}\int_{-k}^{k}\delta_{h}^{n}(x)f(x)\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}x}{\hbox{\rm d}x}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}x$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}x$}}=f(0)

if
0\in\mathring{K}
;

(v)

\lim_{h\to+\infty}\int_{-k}^{k}\delta_{h}^{n}(y-x)f(x)=:\left(\delta_{h}^{n}*_{k}f\right)(y)=f(y)

for all
y\in\mathring{K}
;

(vi)

\lim_{h\to+\infty}\mathcal{F}_{k}\left(\delta_{h}^{n}\right)=1

and
\mathcal{F}_{h}^{-1}(1)=\delta_{h}^{n}
;

(vii)

\mathcal{F}_{h}^{-1}\left(\mathcal{F}_{k}(f)\right)=\mathcal{F}_{h}\left(\mathcal{F}_{k}^{-1}(f)\right)=\delta_{h}^{n}*_{k}f

If
\sigma\ll\rho
, and
k\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}
,
k\gg\partial\rho^{-*}
,
then in the previous properties we can replace the limits with
=_{\rho}

and with
h\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}
sufficiently large.

Proof.

For
(i)
, see (
7.4
). For
(ii)
,
see Lem.
59
. For
(iii)
, see Lem.
60
.
Property
(iv)
is exactly Thm.
61
with
y=0

and considering (
7.3
); the same for
(v)
.
To prove the first equality of
(vi)
, use
(v)

with
f(x)=e^{-i\omega x}
; for the second one, simply compute
\mathcal{F}_{h}^{-1}(1)

and use (
7.4
) for
n=1
. Finally,
(vii)
can
be proved as in (
7.5
).
∎

7.2.
Parseval’s relation, Plancherel’s identity and the uncertainty principle

Theorem 68
.

Let
h\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}
be an infinite
number and set
H:=\left[-h,h\right]^{n}
. Let
f\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GC}^{\infty}\left(K\right)

and
g\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GC}^{\infty}\left(H\right)
. Then

(i)
